Animated Attributes/Overview

MSUltimate - Animated Attributes Documentation

Next-Gen Micro-Interactions, Particle VFX, Motion Graphics, and Audio-Visual Feedback in the Unity Inspector.
Standalone Addon for Unity 6 (6000.x)+.

Overview

MSUltimate Animated Attributes brings dynamic motion, visual alerts, animated transitions, HUD indicators, and micro-interactions into the Unity Inspector. All animations are hardware-accelerated, zero-allocation on repaint, and stripped completely in player runtime builds.

Browse by Category

Real-time frequency analyzers, beat synchronization meters, and sound triggers.

Animated Attributes/Audio & Haptics/[AudioReactivePulse]

[AudioReactivePulse]

Pulses field scale and border glow dynamically in sync with live audio spectrum beats and bass frequencies.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [AudioReactivePulse] analyzes live game audio or editor preview playback and pulses the decorated field in real-time rhythm with low-frequency bass hits. When audio plays, the field scale expands up to MaxScale while radiating an energetic chromatic border glow (BeatColor), driven by an adjustable bass sensitivity factor (BassSensitivity).

The Problem: Audio directors and sound designers frequently tune sound parameters—volume rolloff curves, DSP filter resonance, synth bass parameters—without visual cues on whether beats or drops are registering correctly in the engine.

The Solution: Decorating audio-driven parameters with [AudioReactivePulse]:

  • Real-Time Spectrum Synchronization: Binds inspector controls directly to active audio analysis channels.
  • Rhythmic Bass Detection: Emphasizes kick drums, 808 subs, and low-frequency transients to create an organic, pulsing rhythm.
  • Customizable Beat Aesthetics: Tailor the peak scale expansion (MaxScale) and glowing border shade (BeatColor).
  • Low-Overhead Analysis: Reads shared fast-Fourier-transform (FFT) spectrum buffers with negligible CPU cost.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class RhythmTrackManager : MonoBehaviour
{
    [AudioReactivePulse(sensitivity: 1.8f, BeatColor = "#EC4899")]
    public float masterMusicVolume = 0.85f;
}

6. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class MusicBeatController : MonoBehaviour
{
    [Header("Audio Spectrum Reactivity")]
    [AudioReactivePulse(sensitivity: 2.0f, MaxScale = 1.08f, BeatColor = "#06B6D4")]
    public float bassDropIntensity = 1.0f;

    [Header("Synth Lead Settings")]
    [AudioReactivePulse(sensitivity: 1.2f, BeatColor = "#F59E0B")]
    public float synthPitchCutoff = 800f;
}

7. Stacking & Compatibility Matrix

[Range]SupportedFunctions cleanly on slider rows, pulsing both the handle and label during playback.
Animated Attributes/Audio & Haptics/[EditorSoundEffect]

[EditorSoundEffect]

Plays synthesized auditory cues directly in the Unity Editor when interacting with buttons, toggles, or serializable fields.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [EditorSoundEffect] brings tactile sound design into the Unity Inspector by playing subtle audio synthesizers or chime presets when a field, property, or method button is clicked or modified. Built-in presets include mechanical clicks, futuristic blips, success chimes, and warning alerts.

The Problem: Editor tooling is overwhelmingly visual. Pressing utility buttons, executing builds, or adjusting mission-critical flags provides no auditory confirmation, leading to accidental double-clicks or uncertainty about whether an action executed.

The Solution: Decorating buttons or fields with [EditorSoundEffect]:

  • Auditory Affirmation: Confirms interactions with crisp, satisfying audio feedback.
  • 5 Built-In Presets: Choose from Click, Tick, SciFiBlip, SuccessChime, and WarningBeep.
  • Pitch Modulation: Fine-tune playback Pitch to differentiate actions across a single panel.
  • Method & Button Support: Applies to both fields/properties and [Button] / Method invokers.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class BuildUtility : MonoBehaviour
{
    [EditorSoundEffect(EditorSoundPreset.SuccessChime, Pitch = 1.1f)]
    public void BakeLightingAndNavMesh()
    {
        Debug.Log("Baking completed successfully!");
    }
}

3. Compatibility & Specification

SpecificationValue

Collection CascadingSupported (List<T>, T[], AdvancedList<T>, SerializableDictionary<K, V> via ICollectionElementAttribute)

6. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using System.Collections.Generic;
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.Runtime;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

namespace MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes.Sample
{
    [AddComponentMenu("Maharaja Studio/MSUltimate/Samples/Animated Attributes/Audio And Haptics/Editor Sound Effect Example")]
    public class EditorSoundEffectExample : ModularMonoBehaviour
    {
        // ─── 1. Standalone Tactile Audio Fields ──────────────────────────────────
        [BoxGroup("1. Standalone Tactile Audio Fields", BackgroundColor = "#0F172A", BorderColor = "#2563EB", BorderRadius = 6, MarginBottom = 8)]
        [EditorSoundEffect(EditorSoundPreset.WarningBeep, Pitch = 1.2f)]
        public int emergencyThreatLevel = 3;

        [EditorSoundEffect(EditorSoundPreset.SciFiBlip, Pitch = 0.95f)]
        public string primaryWeaponStatus = "PHOTON-TORPEDOES-ARMED";

        // ─── 2. Inside Class Demonstration ───────────────────────────────────────
        [BoxGroup("2. Inside Class Demonstration", BackgroundColor = "#0D1E36", BorderColor = "#0284C7", BorderRadius = 6, MarginBottom = 8)]
        public AvionicsSwitchModule primaryAvionicsSwitch = new AvionicsSwitchModule();

        [System.Serializable]
        public class AvionicsSwitchModule
        {
            [EditorSoundEffect(EditorSoundPreset.Click, Pitch = 1.1f)]
            public string subsystemName = "Auxiliary Core Coolant";

            [EditorSoundEffect(EditorSoundPreset.Tick, Pitch = 1.3f)]
            public float pressureBar = 14.8f;
        }

        // ─── 3. Direct Collections Cascading Showcase ────────────────────────────
        [BoxGroup("3. Direct Collections Cascading Showcase", BackgroundColor = "#0B182B", BorderColor = "#1D4ED8", BorderRadius = 6, MarginBottom = 8)]
        [InfoBox("Direct collection cascading: [EditorSoundEffect] implements ICollectionElementAttribute to automatically play synthesized tactile sound feedback when modifying individual items in arrays and lists.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]

        [EditorSoundEffect(EditorSoundPreset.Click, 1.2f)]
        public string[] subsystemCallsigns = new string[] { "ALPHA-01", "BRAVO-02", "CHARLIE-03" };

        [EditorSoundEffect(EditorSoundPreset.Tick, 0.9f)]
        public List<float> calibrationFrequencies = new List<float> { 142.5f, 285.0f, 427.5f };

        // ─── 4. Interactive Soundboard Playground ─────────────────────────────────
        [BoxGroup("4. Interactive Soundboard Playground", BackgroundColor = "#13233D", BorderColor = "#38BDF8", BorderRadius = 6)]
        [EditorSoundEffect(EditorSoundPreset.SuccessChime, Pitch = 1.0f)]
        [Button("Deploy Landing Clamps", BackgroundColor = "#10B981", TextColor = "#FFFFFF")]
        public void DeployLandingClamps()
        {
            Debug.Log("[EditorSoundEffect] Deployed landing clamps with audio feedback!");
        }
    }
}

Collection Cascading Matrix

Collection TypeElement TypeSupportedNotes

SerializableDictionary<K, V>Key or Value elementsYesTriggers audio feedback on interacting with dictionary items.

Animated Attributes/Audio & Haptics/[LiveOscilloscopeWave]

[LiveOscilloscopeWave]

Renders a real-time retro CRT phosphor waveform reacting dynamically to value mutations, featuring interactive dragging and dynamic amplitude scaling.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [LiveOscilloscopeWave] transforms standard numeric properties into a live retro cathode-ray tube (CRT) oscilloscope display. A vibrant phosphor trace oscillates horizontally across a customizable canvas, with its amplitude directly driven by the decorated field's value. Users can interactively scrub values by clicking and dragging vertically on the CRT display.

The Problem: Audio synthesizer parameters, pitch modulations, vibration frequencies, and recoil offsets are hard to conceptualize through simple numeric textboxes. Designers need visual wave representations to grasp how adjustments alter waveforms.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields with [LiveOscilloscopeWave]:

  • Real-Time CRT Wave Simulation: Renders an authentic glowing phosphor trace with configurable harmonics and speed.
  • Interactive Drag Scrubbing: Drag directly on the CRT display (IsEditable = true) to scrub amplitudes intuitively.
  • Dynamic Upper Bounds: Bind DynamicMaxValueMember to adapt peak wave scaling to runtime equipment or audio limits.
  • Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute to visualize harmonic lists or audio frequencies inside lists and arrays.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class SynthesizerVoice : MonoBehaviour
{
    [LiveOscilloscopeWave(waveHeight: 50f, phosphorColor: "#39FF14", maxValue: 5f)]
    public float oscillatorFrequency = 2.4f;
}

6. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class AudioDspFilter : MonoBehaviour
{
    [Header("Oscillator Resonance")]
    [LiveOscilloscopeWave(
        waveHeight: 55f, 
        phosphorColor: "#00E5FF", 
        maxValue: 10f, 
        WaveSpeed = 3.5f, 
        WaveComplexity = 4.0f,
        CustomLabel = "LFO Resonance"
    )]
    public float filterResonance = 4.2f;

    [Header("Dynamic Waveform with Member Bound")]
    [LiveOscilloscopeWave(nameof(GetMaxAmplitude), waveHeight: 45f, phosphorColor: "#39FF14")]
    public float modulationAmplitude = 1.8f;

    private float GetMaxAmplitude() => 5.0f;
}

Animated Attributes/Combat & HUD/[BossHealthBar]

[BossHealthBar]

Renders an arcade boss health bar with a lingering damage ghost bar, multi-phase dividers, and interactive click-drag scrubbing.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [BossHealthBar] renders an authentic, multi-layered boss health bar directly inside the Unity Inspector. When health decreases, an underlying amber "ghost bar" lingers at the previous health level and slowly decays (GhostDecaySpeed), mirroring fighting game and souls-like visual mechanics. The bar supports custom phase thresholds (PhaseThresholds), boss titles (BossName), and interactive mouse scrubbing.

The Problem: Balancing boss encounters, phase transitions, and damage thresholds requires constantly adjusting health values. Standard numeric input fields fail to convey the visual impact of phase triggers and health pacing.

The Solution: Decorating boss health fields with [BossHealthBar]:

  • Dual-Layer Ghost Damage Bar: Immediately highlights recent damage decrements with decaying ghost trails.
  • Phase Threshold Dividers: Visual notch markers split the health bar into phases (e.g., 0.75f, 0.5f, 0.25f) for staged boss mechanics.
  • Dynamic Upper Bounds: Support dynamic max health scaling via DynamicMaxHealthMember for scaling encounters.
  • Interactive Drag Editing: Scrub health percentages seamlessly with mouse dragging (IsEditable = true).

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class BossEncounter : MonoBehaviour
{
    [BossHealthBar(maxHealth: 5000f, barHeight: 26f, BossName = "Ignis, Lord of Cinders")]
    public float currentHealth = 3500f;
}

6. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class DragonBoss : MonoBehaviour
{
    [Header("Encounter Max Health")]
    public float maximumDragonHealth = 12000f;

    [Header("Active Boss Health")]
    [BossHealthBar(
        nameof(maximumDragonHealth), 
        barHeight: 28f, 
        BossName = "Ancient Wyrm of the Peak",
        BarColor = "#DC2626",
        GhostColor = "#F59E0B",
        GhostDecaySpeed = 0.4f,
        PhaseThresholds = new float[] { 0.70f, 0.35f }
    )]
    public float currentHealth = 8400f;
}

Animated Attributes/Combat & HUD/[CombatComboCounter]

[CombatComboCounter]

Displays an arcade combo counter with animated rank badges (SSS to C) and hit-shake feedback on numeric combat fields.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [CombatComboCounter] transforms numeric combo fields into an arcade-style combo visualizer. Each combo increment triggers a visceral screen-shake displacement (ShakeStrength) and dynamically updates an animated arcade tier rank badge (from C up to SSS rank). It includes timeout decay indicators (TimeoutSeconds) and supports interactive increment clicking and dragging.

The Problem: Tuning combat flow—such as combo timeouts, rank thresholds, and hit-counter multipliers—in character action games requires testing how combo states feel and escalate during rapid combat sequences.

The Solution: Decorating hit-counter properties with [CombatComboCounter]:

  • Dynamic Arcade Rank Badges: Automatically calculates and transitions badges (C, B, A, S, SS, SSS) based on current hits.
  • Hit-Shake Physics: Vibrates the combo widget with physical impact displacement on every hit registered.
  • Decay Timeout Tracking: Monitors inactivity decay intervals before the combo streak drops.
  • Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute for tracking party combo chains or multi-character hits.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class CharacterCombat : MonoBehaviour
{
    [CombatComboCounter(timeout: 4.0f, maxCombo: 999)]
    public int currentHitStreak = 42;
}

[ManaShieldBubble]

Renders an undulating hexagonal forcefield shield with chromatic ripples around defensive or energy fields.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [ManaShieldBubble] wraps the decorated inspector field in an animated sci-fi or fantasy hexagonal energy barrier. The forcefield grid features undulating ambient luminescence and emits expanding ripples across its honeycomb cells whenever the user hovers or interacts with the control.

The Problem: Magic shields, protective barriers, energy conduits, and sci-fi deflector fields look identical to standard health or armor stats in generic inspectors, obscuring the magical or technological nature of the mechanic.

The Solution: Decorating protective stats with [ManaShieldBubble]:

  • Animated Honeycomb Grid: Procedurally renders a glowing hexagonal tessellation directly behind and around the field.
  • Interactive Energy Ripples: Clicking or modifying values propagates concentric wave ripples (RippleSpeed) through the shield lattice.
  • Customizable Barrier Palette: Modify barrier theme color (ShieldColor) from cyan mana shields to amber kinetic barriers or purple void shields.
  • Adjustable Cell Geometry: Scale individual hexagonal cell radius (HexSize) to fit narrow or wide inspector layouts.

Animated Attributes/Combat & HUD/[RPGRarityGlow]

[RPGRarityGlow]

Wraps an inspector field in an animated RPG rarity aura with floating particle sparks and decorative corner badges.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [RPGRarityGlow] encloses the decorated field within an animated, color-coded rarity border aura that reflects classic RPG loot tiers—from Common grey to Legendary gold and Mythic cosmic violet. It features floating ambient particle motes and optional corner insignia badges (ShowCornerBadges).

The Problem: In loot-heavy games, inventory ScriptableObjects and item prefabs are difficult to distinguish at a glance when inspecting data folders. Common consumables and ultra-rare artifacts look identical in standard grey inspector panels.

The Solution: Applying [RPGRarityGlow] to item data:

  • Instant Tier Recognition: Instantly visually communicates item rarity using standard industry color conventions.
  • 6 Built-In Rarity Tiers: Out-of-the-box support for Common, Uncommon, Rare, Epic, Legendary, and Mythic.
  • Floating Ambient Sparks: Gentle particle sparks drift upward along the aura borders for high-tier items.
  • Corner Badges & Scalable Intensity: Toggle ornate corner gems (ShowCornerBadges) and fine-tune AuraIntensity.

2. Quick Start

Animated Attributes/Core Animated & Base/[AnimatedConditionalHighlight]

[AnimatedConditionalHighlight]

Triggers an attention-grabbing animated pulsing highlight background when a specified boolean condition method evaluates to true.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: Monitors a custom boolean method callback at regular intervals (CheckInterval). When the condition returns true, it activates a rhythmic pulsing background glow (HighlightColor) behind the field, drawing the developer's immediate focus to critical states (e.g. low ammo, dangerous health thresholds, unsaved configs).

The Problem: Static inspector warnings easily blend into busy components and get overlooked during rapid iteration or playtesting.

The Solution: Add [AnimatedConditionalHighlight] to any field:

  • Dynamic Reactive Pulse: Automatically pulses only when game state or field configuration warrants attention.
  • Configurable Speed & Color: Fine-tune oscillation speed (PulseSpeed) and highlight hex color.
  • Interval Throttling: Periodic evaluation (CheckInterval = 500ms) ensures negligible CPU overhead.
  • Clean Auto-Dismissal: The pulsing animation stops cleanly the moment the condition returns false.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class TurretController : MonoBehaviour
{
    [AnimatedConditionalHighlight(nameof(IsAmmoDepleted), HighlightColor = "#FF000055", PulseSpeed = 2f)]
    public int currentAmmo = 0;

    private bool IsAmmoDepleted() => currentAmmo <= 0;
}

Animated Attributes/Core Animated & Base/[AnimatedDrawer]

[AnimatedDrawer]

Foundational base class for animated custom field drawers that replace default property rendering with dynamic interactive controls.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: Serves as the abstract base class and category assigner for animated primary drawers in MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes. Attributes inheriting from AnimatedDrawerAttribute are categorized under AttributeCategory.PrimaryDrawer, establishing them as the primary rendering authority for the decorated field while providing hardware-accelerated UI Toolkit animation loops.

The Problem: Creating custom interactive animated controls (like sliding toggles, animated scrub bars, or vector-morphing checkboxes) in standard Unity requires complex PropertyDrawer overrides that frequently break layout stacking or drop frames.

The Solution: Extend AnimatedDrawerAttribute when building custom animated field controls:

  • Primary Control Authority: Replaces default IMGUI/UI Toolkit field rendering with a bespoke animated element.
  • Micro-Interaction Pipeline: Manages gesture transitions, mouse hover, drag scrub, and state animations.
  • Powers Next-Gen Drawers: Foundational base for [InteractiveToggle], [AnimatedProgressBar], [AnimatedMarquee], and [MorphingCheckbox].

2. Quick Start

csharp
using System;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

// Creating a custom animated knob drawer
[AttributeUsage(AttributeTargets.Field | AttributeTargets.Property, AllowMultiple = false, Inherited = true)]
public class AnimatedKnobAttribute : AnimatedDrawerAttribute
{
    public float TurnSpeed { get; set; } = 150f;
}

The Solution: Add [AnimatedHover] to any field or property:

  • Tactile Responsiveness: Subtle scale lifts (1.02x) and color shifts reward user interaction.

Animated Attributes/Core Animated & Base/[AnimatedProgressBar]

[AnimatedProgressBar]

Renders a smoothly animating progress fill bar directly underneath a numeric field with interactive scrubbing, dynamic limits, threshold alerts, and text format tokens.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: Enhances numeric float and integer fields with a hardware-accelerated animated progress meter. Features smooth tweening transitions upon value changes, click-and-drag mouse scrub editing directly on the bar, dynamic min/max limits linked to class members, custom label formatting tokens, and automatic threshold warning color shifts (e.g. low health alert).

The Problem: Standard numeric inspector fields (or static IMGUI EditorGUI.ProgressBar) provide zero tactile feedback, do not support dragging to scrub values, cannot smoothly animate value transitions, and require hardcoded static min/max bounds.

The Solution: Add [AnimatedProgressBar] to any numeric field:

  • Smooth Fill Transitions: Animated tween fills (AnimationDuration = 300ms) respond gracefully to changes.
  • Interactive Mouse Scrubbing: Click or drag directly across the bar to adjust values (IsEditable = true).
  • Dynamic Limits: Bind MaxValue to another property (e.g. [AnimatedProgressBar(nameof(maxHealth))]).
  • Low Threshold Alarm: Shifts to warning red (LowThresholdColor) when ratio drops below LowThreshold.
  • Rich Text Tokens: Custom label formatting supports {value}, {min}, {max}, and {percent} tokens.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class PlayerHealth : MonoBehaviour
{
    [AnimatedProgressBar(0f, 100f, FillColor = "#2ecc71", ShowValue = true, ShowPercentage = true)]
    public float currentHealth = 85f;
}

Animated Attributes/Core Animated & Base/[Animated]

[Animated]

Foundational base class for all decorative animation attributes in the MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes addon, integrating micro-interactions into the PreDecorator pipeline.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: Serves as the abstract base class and category assigner for all decorative animation attributes in the MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes addon. Attributes extending AnimatedAttribute are classified under AttributeCategory.PreDecorator, equipping them with high-performance UI Toolkit transition hooks, zero-allocation animation lifecycles, and automatic editor stripping in player builds.

The Problem: Adding animations, micro-interactions, or reactive hover states to custom Unity inspectors typically requires hundreds of lines of boilerplate USS stylesheets, visual element transitions, and manual editor ticking logic.

The Solution: Extend AnimatedAttribute when crafting custom animated inspector behaviors:

  • PreDecorator Lifecycle: Seamlessly attaches visual transitions to target fields.
  • Hardware-Accelerated: Backed by modern UI Toolkit animation loops with smooth 60fps refresh.
  • Zero Player Build Footprint: Stripped automatically during standalone build compilation.
  • Foundation of Animated Attributes: Powers attributes like [AnimatedHover], [AnimatedFocus], [AnimatedSlideIn], and [PulseAttention].

2. Quick Start

csharp
using System;

public class CustomPulseAttribute : AnimatedAttribute
{
    public float PulseDuration { get; set; } = 300f;
}

Animated Attributes/Core Animated & Base/[MagneticHover]

[MagneticHover]

Applies a spring-based magnetic pull physics effect that smoothly attracts the inspector element toward the mouse cursor on hover.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: Introduces playful, organic physics interactions into the Inspector. When the mouse cursor moves within MagneticRadius (in pixels) of the field, the element is drawn magnetically toward the cursor up to MaxPullDistance, springing back into place (SpringForce) when the mouse leaves.

The Problem: Standard UI elements feel dead and completely disconnected from the mouse cursor, making the editor experience rigid and dry.

The Solution: Add [MagneticHover] to key fields or buttons:

  • Organic Physics Micro-Interaction: High-polish spring magnetic pull responds dynamically to cursor proximity.
  • Configurable Magnetic Field: Tune attraction radius (MagneticRadius = 150px) and maximum displacement pull.
  • Natural Spring Snapback: Damped spring return restores element alignment instantly upon cursor departure.
  • Ideal for Action Buttons & Sliders: Makes key interactive settings feel magnetic and enticing.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class SpecialAction : MonoBehaviour
{
    [MagneticHover(MagneticRadius = 120f, MaxPullDistance = 12f, SpringForce = 0.25f)]
    public bool triggerSpecialEvent = false;
}

Animated Attributes/Core Animated & Base/[MorphingCheckbox]

[MorphingCheckbox]

Renders boolean fields with a vector-drawn icon that smoothly morphs vector paths between checkmark and cross states with spring animation.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: Replaces the standard binary checkbox with a vector path visual that smoothly interpolates and morphs its geometry: morphing from a red "" when false into a crisp green "" when true. Powered by vector stroke interpolation with customizable colors, line thickness, and transition duration.

The Problem: Standard Unity checkboxes swap sprites abruptly without any intermediate animation, feeling mechanical and uninspired.

The Solution: Add [MorphingCheckbox] to any boolean field:

  • Smooth Vector Path Morphing: The stroke smoothly bends and animates from a cross into a checkmark.
  • Color Transitions: Interpolates smoothly between UncheckedColor (red) and CheckedColor (green).
  • Line Weight Control: Configure stroke width (LineThickness = 3px) for delicate or bold vector graphics.
  • Collection Elements: Renders morphing icons for boolean items inside lists and arrays (ICollectionElementAttribute).

[ParticleBurst]

Spawns an animated 2D canvas particle burst explosion (confetti or sparks) with physics gravity and velocity spread when a field is clicked or modified.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: Spawns a delightful, celebratory mini-particle simulation directly inside the Inspector window whenever the decorated field is clicked or changed. Particles erupt outward with customizable velocity (Spread), gravity acceleration (Gravity), color (ParticleColor), count (ParticleCount), and lifetime (BurstDuration).

The Problem: Completing major milestones, clicking critical build buttons, or discovering rare items in inspector data tables feels mundane without visual celebration.

The Solution: Add [ParticleBurst] to any field or button:

  • Celebratory Feedback: Rewarding particle sparks / confetti explosions celebrate crucial interactions.
  • 2D Canvas Physics: Particles simulate genuine 2D ballistic physics with velocity dispersion and downward gravity.
  • Particle Customization: Configure particle count (e.g. 25), spread radius, and spark color (#FFD700 gold).

Animated Attributes/Core Animated & Base/[PulseAttention]

[PulseAttention]

Pulses the field scale and color cyclically to draw user attention to critical or unconfigured inspector settings.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [PulseAttention] animates a cyclic, breathing pulse across the decorated field's scale and background or border color. It draws immediate visual priority to mandatory configuration fields, warning thresholds, or newly added inspector options without requiring disruptive modal dialogs.

The Problem: Complex components often contain critical fields that designers inadvertently overlook during setup (e.g., missing essential references or unassigned gameplay multipliers). Static labels blend into long inspector layouts, leading to runtime null references or misconfigured mechanics.

The Solution: Applying [PulseAttention] to an inspector field:

  • Draws Immediate Focus: A rhythmic, pulsating breathing animation commands designer attention immediately upon opening the inspector.
  • Configurable Scale & Color: Independently toggle and fine-tune scaling bounds (MinScale, MaxScale) and color oscillations (PulseColor, BaseColor).
  • Adjustable Frequency: Modulate oscillation speed via Speed (cycles per second) to create subtle guidance or urgent alerts.
  • Performant & Zero Allocations: Built directly on UI Toolkit's scheduler, ensuring smooth 60fps rendering without garbage collection spikes.

Animated Attributes/Core Animated & Base/[RevealOnHover]

[RevealOnHover]

Fades an inspector field to a reduced opacity until the mouse cursor hovers over it, minimizing visual clutter in data-dense components.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [RevealOnHover] keeps secondary, advanced, or auxiliary inspector fields at low opacity (HiddenOpacity) until the user hovers their mouse cursor over the control. Upon mouse entry, the field smoothly transitions to full opacity over RevealDuration milliseconds.

The Problem: Extensive game entities often feature dozens of niche debug settings, technical tuning variables, and secondary overrides. When all fields are displayed at full prominence, inspectors become overwhelming and tedious to navigate.

The Solution: Decorating secondary fields with [RevealOnHover]:

  • Reduces Cognitive Load: Dimmed fields subtly fade into the background, allowing primary gameplay variables to stand out.
  • Fluid Transition Curves: Smooth fade-in and fade-out animations provide a sleek, polished AAA interface experience.
  • Configurable Ghost Tinting: Adjust the dormant opacity level (HiddenOpacity) or apply a custom resting tint (HiddenColor).
  • Interactive Accessibility: The field remains fully interactive even when dimmed; hovering immediately restores full visual prominence.

Animated Attributes/Core Animated & Base/[ShakeOnError]

[ShakeOnError]

Shakes an inspector field horizontally and flashes an error tint whenever validation fails or required values are missing.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [ShakeOnError] provides tactile, physics-inspired error feedback directly inside the Unity Inspector. When an input fails validation—either because an object/string is left empty or a custom validation callback evaluates to false—the field executes a rapid horizontal camera-shake vibration accompanied by a red error flash (ErrorColor).

The Problem: Static validation messages or tiny warning icons can easily be ignored in busy inspectors, causing developers to run play mode with missing assets or invalid numeric ranges and trigger avoidable crashes.

The Solution: Applying [ShakeOnError] to input fields:

  • Instant Visceral Feedback: The horizontal rumble directly tells the user that their entered value is invalid.
  • Custom Validation Logic: Link a custom C# boolean method via ValidationMethod or rely on built-in non-empty checks via ValidateNotEmpty.
  • Configurable Vibration Dynamics: Fine-tune ShakeIntensity (pixels), ShakeSpeed (Hz), and Duration (ms) to match your UI feel.
  • Visual Alert Tinting: Emphasizes invalid state with an accent background flash (ErrorColor).

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class ProfileSetup : MonoBehaviour
{
    [ShakeOnError(ValidateNotEmpty = true, ShakeIntensity = 6f)]
    public string playerName;
}

csharp
public ShakeOnErrorAttribute()

Initializes error vibration with 5px amplitude, 25Hz oscillation, and 400ms duration. Set validation rules and parameters via object initializers.

5. Properties & Customization Table

PropertyTypeDefaultDescription
ValidationMethodstring""Name of an instance method returning a bool (false triggers error shake).
ValidateNotEmptybooltrueWhen true, automatically shakes if an object reference is null or string is empty.
ShakeIntensityfloat5fMaximum horizontal displacement offset in pixels during the shake.
ShakeSpeedfloat25fOscillation frequency of the horizontal vibration in cycles per second.

6. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class NetworkRoomConfig : MonoBehaviour
{
    [Header("Mandatory Credentials")]
    [ShakeOnError(ValidateNotEmpty = true, ShakeIntensity = 8f, Duration = 500f)]
    public string serverEndpoint = "https://api.game.com";

    [Header("Port Validation")]
    [ShakeOnError(
        ValidationMethod = nameof(ValidatePort), 
        ShakeIntensity = 4f, 
        ErrorColor = "#e74c3c44"
    )]
    public int networkPort = 7777;

    private bool ValidatePort()
    {
        return networkPort >= 1024 && networkPort <= 65535;
    }
}

  • Unobtrusive User Experience: Immediately clears the moment the user clicks the field or enters real data.
  • Zero String Allocations in Runtime: Runs strictly inside the editor UI Toolkit layer without generating runtime garbage.

Animated Attributes/Core Animated & Base/[ValueChangeFlash]

[ValueChangeFlash]

Flashes an energetic color accent and scale bump whenever an inspector field value is altered or updated by tools.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [ValueChangeFlash] listens for value mutations on a serialized field and triggers an instantaneous animated visual flash. Whenever the value changes—via keyboard typing, slider dragging, undo/redo operations, or script assignment—the control produces a transient background color flash (FlashColor) and a tactile scale bump (ScaleBump).

The Problem: When balancing gameplay stats, scrubbing live parameters in Play Mode, or running editor utility scripts, it is hard to tell whether a setting was modified or if an automation method actually touched a field.

The Solution: Applying [ValueChangeFlash] to fields:

  • Instant Mutation Acknowledgment: Confirms changes visually with a crisp, momentary highlight flash.
  • Micro-Scale Dynamics: Applies a subtle, responsive scale bump (ScaleBump) that gives data editing a tactile, physical quality.
  • Customizable Flash Palette: Pick any hex color and opacity (e.g., emerald green #4CAF5055 for rewards, gold #FFD70055 for levels).
  • Automation Visibility: Instantly illuminates values when modified programmatically by custom inspector buttons or external tooling.

    public float[] elementalResistances = new float[] { 25.0f, 50.0f, 75.0f };

    [ValueChangeFlash(FlashColor = "#38BDF877", FlashDuration = 500f)]
    public List<string> activeQuestLog = new List<string> { "Find ancient relic", "Defeat forest guardian" };
}

Animated Attributes/Data Visualizers/[AnimatedDialGauge]

[AnimatedDialGauge]

Displays a circular dial gauge with a damped animated needle, glowing arc fill, dynamic bounds, and interactive needle scrubbing.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [AnimatedDialGauge] replaces conventional numeric textboxes or linear sliders with an analog circular dial gauge. A smooth spring-damped needle (NeedleDamping) points along a glowing circular arc (ArcColor) representing current numeric progress between MinValue and MaxValue. Users can click and drag angularly along the dial face to interactively scrub values.

The Problem: Speedometers, tachometers, steering angles, power gauges, and pressure meters are fundamentally circular in nature. Linear horizontal progress bars fail to convey the tactile feel of rotary automotive or aviation meters.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields with [AnimatedDialGauge]:

  • Analog Spring-Damped Needle: Smoothly interpolates toward target values with authentic mechanical inertia.
  • Interactive Angular Dragging: Click and scrub anywhere on the dial face (IsEditable = true) to rotate the needle.
  • Dynamic Upper & Lower Bounds: Bind DynamicMaxValueMember and DynamicMinValueMember for contextual dial ranges.
  • Value & Percentage Overlays: Formatted numeric readout (ValueFormat) or percentage text displayed cleanly below the dial.
  • Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute for multi-dial motor arrays or engine cylinder telemetries.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class VehicleSpeedometer : MonoBehaviour
{
    [AnimatedDialGauge(min: 0f, max: 240f, size: 90f, NeedleColor = "#EF4444")]
    public float currentSpeedKmh = 120f;
}

[AnimatedLiquidBar]

Renders a progress bar with undulating fluid wave ripples, rising micro-bubbles, and interactive level scrubbing.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [AnimatedLiquidBar] elevates numeric progress bars into an organic liquid container. The fill level features procedural surface waves (WaveAmplitude, WaveSpeed), a lighter surface wave crest (SurfaceWaveColor), and gentle rising effervescent bubbles. Users can click and drag horizontally on the fluid container to scrub levels interactively.

The Problem: Alchemical flasks, potion volumes, fuel tanks, stamina reservoirs, and water tanks feel static when represented by rigid, mechanical progress bars. Standard bars fail to evoke the presence of fluid dynamics.

The Solution: Decorating liquid or resource properties with [AnimatedLiquidBar]:

  • Fluid Wave Simulation: Sine-wave surface crests ripple continuously to simulate organic fluid motion.
  • Surface Crest Highlights: A two-tone fluid aesthetic with a brighter wave foam line (SurfaceWaveColor) on top of the main body (LiquidColor).
  • Interactive Drag Scrubbing: Drag directly along the fluid bar (IsEditable = true) to fill or deplete fluid levels.
  • Dynamic Capacity Limits: Bind DynamicMaxValueMember and DynamicMinValueMember for expandable flasks or tanks.
  • Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute for potion belts or chemical multi-tank inventories.

Animated Attributes/Data Visualizers/[AnimatedLiveCurve]

[AnimatedLiveCurve]

Renders an animated glowing point tracer traveling continuously along an AnimationCurve preview with luminous comet trails.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [AnimatedLiveCurve] turns standard Unity AnimationCurve fields into an animated live trajectory preview. A luminous point tracer (TracerColor) glides continuously along the evaluated curve shape at an adjustable speed (TracerSpeed), leaving a glowing decay trail (TrailLength) behind it.

The Problem: Designing ease-in/ease-out curves, camera recoil paths, bounce curves, or weapon velocity envelopes in the standard static curve field provides no sense of speed, easing acceleration, or temporal cadence without repeatedly entering Play Mode.

The Solution: Decorating AnimationCurve properties with [AnimatedLiveCurve]:

  • Real-Time Temporal Preview: Immediately visualizes where the curve accelerates, slows down, or loops over time.
  • Luminous Particle Tracer: Renders a glowing point tracer accompanied by a decaying comet tail (TrailLength).
  • Fully Preserved Curve Editing: The built-in Unity AnimationCurve drawer remains fully accessible and interactive right above or alongside the preview.
  • Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute for multi-stage curve sequences or serialized animation blend profiles.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class CameraShakeProfile : MonoBehaviour
{
    [AnimatedLiveCurve(height: 52f, tracerColor: "#F43F5E")]
    public AnimationCurve traumaDecayCurve = AnimationCurve.EaseInOut(0f, 1f, 1f, 0f);

Animated Attributes/Data Visualizers/[AudioEqualizer]

[AudioEqualizer]

Displays animated spectrum equalizer bars driven by numeric audio or volume properties, with peak cap markers and interactive scrubbing.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [AudioEqualizer] visualizes volume, decibel levels, or audio channel gains using an animated multi-column spectrum equalizer. Vertical bars fluctuate dynamically (AnimationSpeed) with decaying peak cap markers (PeakColor) that trace transient sound spikes. Users can scrub levels interactively by clicking and dragging anywhere across the equalizer container.

The Problem: Audio volumes and channel gains represented by flat numeric floats or standard sliders feel clinical and disconnected from acoustic hardware. Sound designers cannot easily gauge whether a channel has high dynamic headroom or if levels are clipped.

The Solution: Decorating volume or gain properties with [AudioEqualizer]:

  • Hardware-Style Spectrum Columns: Renders a customizable bank of vertical VU bars (BarCount, BarHeight).
  • Peak Hold Indicators: Decaying cap markers (PeakColor) visually capture maximum volume spikes.
  • Interactive Drag Scrubbing: Drag anywhere on the equalizer widget (IsEditable = true) to adjust volumes fluidly.
  • Dynamic Cap Limits: Bind DynamicMaxValueMember to adapt volume scaling to dynamic audio busses.
  • Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute for multi-channel audio mixer buses and soundbanks.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class SoundManager : MonoBehaviour
{
    [AudioEqualizer(barCount: 10, barColor: "#10B981")]
    public float masterVolume = 85f;
}

Animated Attributes/Data Visualizers/[RadialHeatmap]

[RadialHeatmap]

Renders a circular gauge that dynamically shifts color from cool blue to pulsing hot red as values rise toward critical thresholds.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [RadialHeatmap] provides a thermal visual indicator for temperature, danger levels, resource stress, or engine heat. As values rise from MinValue to MaxValue, the circular arc smoothly interpolates across three distinct thermal tiers: CoolColor (blue), WarmColor (amber), and HotColor (red). When the value exceeds CriticalThreshold, the gauge engages an urgent pulsing heartbeat glow.

The Problem: Heat buildup, radiation exposure, core temperature, and weapon overheating mechanics become dangerous only near extreme limits. Flat numerical fields provide no peripheral sense of thermal escalation or impending crisis.

The Solution: Decorating thermal properties with [RadialHeatmap]:

  • Dynamic 3-Tone Thermal Palette: Automatically blends between CoolColor, WarmColor, and HotColor.
  • Urgent Critical Warning Pulse: Activates a rhythmic warning pulse as soon as values exceed CriticalThreshold.
  • Circular Interactive Scrubbing: Drag directly along the circular ring (IsEditable = true) to modulate thermal levels.
  • Dynamic Upper & Lower Bounds: Bind DynamicMaxValueMember and DynamicMinValueMember for contextual limits.
  • Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute for multi-core reactors or thruster clusters.

2. Quick Start

csharp

using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

    [RadialHeatmap(min: 0f, max: 100f, CriticalThreshold = 85f)]
    public float coreTemperature = 45f;

Animated Attributes/Data Visualizers/[StepProgressor]

[StepProgressor]

Renders a multi-step milestone progress strip with glowing connectors, node badges, and interactive step selection.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [StepProgressor] transforms discrete integers or Enum stages into a modern multi-stage milestone stepper. It renders connected circular step nodes (StepSize) linked by illuminated connector bars. Completed stages illuminate in ActiveColor, unreached stages rest in InactiveColor, and the currently active node pulses with an ambient aura (PulseGlowColor). Users can click or scrub across milestone nodes to change active steps interactively.

The Problem: Tutorial flows, quest objectives, crafting pipelines, and onboarding phases are sequential state machines. Standard enum dropdowns or raw integer fields provide no geographic representation of where the entity currently sits within the larger progression sequence.

The Solution: Decorating integer or Enum progress fields with [StepProgressor]:

  • Visual Milestone Flow: Clearly illustrates sequence completion from initial step to finale.
  • Current Step Pulse Glow: Emphasizes the currently active milestone with a breathing aura (PulseGlowColor).
  • Interactive Step Selection: Click directly on milestone node circles (IsEditable = true) to advance or rewind states.
  • Custom Stage Titles: Provide human-readable stage descriptions via StepLabels.
  • Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute for quest tracker lists or multi-character tutorial logs.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;

[HeartbeatAlert]

Animates a double-pulse heartbeat scale and red warning glow when vital numeric resources drop below critical safety thresholds.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HeartbeatAlert] provides an urgent, physical warning cadence whenever a numeric resource (health, battery, oxygen, shield) drops below CriticalThreshold. It simulates a biological double-pulse cardiac heartbeat (lub-dub) through rapid micro-scale contractions (ScaleBump) and synchronized crimson vascular glow pulses (GlowColor), cycling at an authentic beats-per-minute cadence (BPM).

The Problem: When balancing survival mechanics, roguelikes, or horror games, designers easily miss that initial health setups or enemy damage tuning leaves the player in immediate danger upon spawning. Static warning labels fail to deliver visceral urgency.

The Solution: Decorating vital resource properties with [HeartbeatAlert]:

  • Biological Double-Pulse Rhythm: Renders a realistic anatomical heartbeat rhythm that instantly triggers urgency.
  • Dynamic Threshold Trigger: Dormant during healthy states; automatically awakens the instant values dip below CriticalThreshold.
  • Configurable Cadence & Color: Fine-tune tempo via BPM, accent color via GlowColor, and scale pulse strength via ScaleBump.
  • Low-Overhead UI Toolkit Scheduling: Automatically deschedules ticking timers when above threshold or hidden, consuming zero overhead during normal operation.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class PlayerVitality : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HeartbeatAlert(criticalThreshold: 20f, bpm: 80f)]
    public float currentHealth = 15f;
}

Animated Attributes/Feedback & Alerts/[ImpactRecoil]

[ImpactRecoil]

Snaps the inspector field backward with physical recoil kick displacement upon value changes, recovering smoothly via simulated spring physics.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [ImpactRecoil] delivers punchy physical feedback inside the Unity Inspector whenever a field's value changes. It simulates a mechanical or ballistic recoil kick by displacing the entire field horizontally (RecoilPixels) instantly upon value mutation, then smoothly interpolating back to rest over a configurable recovery duration (RecoveryDuration) using damped kinetic restitution.

The Problem: Tweaking combat weapon stats, ammunition counters, impact forces, or damage multipliers in the default Unity Inspector feels passive and disconnected from physical impact. Designers cannot easily perceive when rapid mutations or automated gameplay balance scripts fire during Play Mode tests.

The Solution: Decorating numeric or state fields with [ImpactRecoil]:

  • Tactile Kinematic Feedback: Visually "kicks" the UI field backward upon each value delta, mimicking weapon slide cycles and heavy mechanical switches.
  • Directional Recoil Control: Choose leftward (RecoilLeft = true) or rightward kick directions to match visual layout semantics and HUD alignments.
  • Customizable Restitution Curve: Adjust recovery duration (RecoveryDuration) and displacement distance (RecoilPixels) for anything from subtle micro-kicks to violent shotgun blasts.
  • Zero Runtime Overhead: Implemented via hardware-accelerated UI Toolkit transforms and tween scheduling that idles at 0% CPU when values are resting.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class WeaponMechanics : MonoBehaviour
{
    [ImpactRecoil(recoilPixels: 10f, recoveryDuration: 200f)]
    public int currentAmmoCount = 30;
}

Animated Attributes/Feedback & Alerts/[ThresholdSpikeFlash]

[ThresholdSpikeFlash]

Emits an instantaneous shockwave ripple and flash when a numeric field crosses defined threshold milestone boundaries.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [ThresholdSpikeFlash] attaches dynamic boundary monitoring to numeric properties in the Unity Inspector. As soon as a serialized field or property crosses one of the predefined threshold boundaries (Thresholds), the attribute triggers a radiating shockwave ripple and chromatic flash (FlashColor) across the property row for the specified duration (FlashDuration).

The Problem: Numeric balancing frequently involves non-linear milestones—such as engine overheating past 100°C, speed barrier crossings, armor degradation phases, or boss enrage trigger percentages. Without visual feedback, developers and level designers must constantly reference documentation spreadsheets to know when a tuned variable has triggered critical gameplay phase gates.

The Solution: Decorating milestone variables with [ThresholdSpikeFlash]:

  • Instant Milestone Shockwaves: Dispatches an immediate visual ripple effect when the field value crosses from below to above (or vice-versa) any threshold milestone.
  • Multiple Arbitrary Thresholds: Configure single or multiple boundary checkpoints (e.g., 25f, 50f, 75f, 100f) via params float[] in the constructor.
  • Vibrant Alert Customization: Tailor the shockwave hue (FlashColor) and transition duration (FlashDuration) to represent warnings, level gates, or danger thresholds.
  • Efficient Transition Polling: Utilizes lightweight property change subscriptions within UI Toolkit, executing zero tick loops when the inspector field is idle.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class ReactorCore : MonoBehaviour
{
    [ThresholdSpikeFlash(50f, 80f, 100f)]
    public float temperatureCelsius = 45f;
}

6. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using System.Collections.Generic;
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.Runtime;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class ReactorCoreTelemetry : ModularMonoBehaviour
{
    [System.Serializable]
    public class AuxiliaryGeneratorCell
    {
        public string generatorName = "Sub-Level Fusion Cell 03";

        [ThresholdSpikeFlash(50f, 100f, 150f, FlashColor = "#EC4899", FlashDuration = 400f)]
        public float powerOutputMw = 85f;
    }

    [BoxGroup("1. Standalone Shockwaves")]
    [ThresholdSpikeFlash(25f, 50f, 75f, 100f, FlashColor = "#10B981", FlashDuration = 500f)]
    public float capacitorChargeLevel = 45f;

    [ThresholdSpikeFlash(80f, 100f, FlashColor = "#EF4444", FlashDuration = 600f)]
    public float turbineLoadTemperature = 65f;

    [BoxGroup("2. Nested Class Showcase")]
    public AuxiliaryGeneratorCell backupCell = new AuxiliaryGeneratorCell();

    [BoxGroup("3. Direct Collections Cascading")]
    [InfoBox("Milestone shockwaves radiate across individual collection rows as each element crosses defined boundaries.")]
    [ThresholdSpikeFlash(50f, 100f, 150f, FlashColor = "#EF4444", FlashDuration = 500f)]
    public float[] reactorZoneTemperatures = new float[] { 45f, 95f, 140f };

    [ThresholdSpikeFlash(25f, 50f, 75f, FlashColor = "#10B981", FlashDuration = 450f)]
    public List<int> subsystemBatteryPercentages = new List<int> { 20, 48, 70 };
}

Animated Attributes/Feedback & Alerts/[WarningStrobe]

[WarningStrobe]

Renders alternating high-visibility emergency hazard warning stripes that pulse rhythmically across invalid or critical inspector fields.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [WarningStrobe] overlays high-contrast, industrial hazard hazard stripes behind or around inspector controls. The stripes oscillate rhythmically between two customizable hex colors (StrobeColor1, StrobeColor2) at a designated frequency (StrobeSpeed in Hertz). It can be configured to pulse permanently (AlwaysActive = true) or activate upon critical error conditions.

The Problem: In expansive projects with hundreds of configurable fields, missing an essential reference, setting an unsafe multiplier, or overlooking an uncalibrated threshold can cause runtime failure. Subdued text warnings and subtle error icons frequently blend into the surrounding dark grey editor background.

The Solution: Decorating critical or hazardous fields with [WarningStrobe]:

  • Industrial Hazard Aesthetic: Instantly demands developer attention with alternating diagonal hazard warning bars.
  • Dynamic Frequency Tuning: Regulate pulse oscillation tempo via StrobeSpeed (Hz) for subtle reminders or frantic emergency strobes.
  • Dual-Color Customization: Configure contrasting warning duotones (e.g., hazard amber/black, radioactive yellow/charcoal, or emergency crimson/white).
  • Persistent or Conditional Modes: Toggle between unconditional always-on display (AlwaysActive) and event-triggered alerting.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class SecurityConsole : MonoBehaviour
{
    [WarningStrobe(color1: "#EF4444", strobeSpeed: 3.5f)]
    public bool lockdownProtocolActive = true;
}

    {
        "ALARM-901: COOLANT VENT VALVE STUCK OPEN",

    };

Animated Attributes/Gamification/[ConfettiCannon]

[ConfettiCannon]

Launches a celebratory 2D physics-simulated confetti burst across the inspector on button clicks, method invocations, or value changes.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [ConfettiCannon] brings dopamine-rich celebratory feedback into the Unity editor by spawning a multi-colored fountain of fluttering 2D confetti particles. The simulation integrates realistic initial velocity (InitialVelocity), downward gravity (Gravity), rotational aerodynamic flutter, and a timed fadeout lifecycle (BurstDuration).

The Problem: Completing long editor tasks—such as baking navmeshes, generating procedural dungeons, verifying level data, or leveling up test characters—often concludes with anti-climactic static console logs or silent field updates. This lacks emotional reward and makes verifying batch operations dull.

The Solution: Decorating methods, buttons, or achievement fields with [ConfettiCannon]:

  • Celebratory 2D Physics: Simulates authentic fluttering paper ribbon physics with gravity and drag right on the editor canvas.
  • Versatile Target Application: Decorate inspector action methods ([Button]), numeric milestone fields, or property setters.
  • Fine-Tuned Particle Dynamics: Control particle count (ParticleCount), launch arc (InitialVelocity), gravity pull (Gravity), and animation duration (BurstDuration).
  • High-Performance Vector Rendering: Rendered directly using lightweight UI Toolkit custom draw commands, leaving zero residual gameobjects or memory leaks.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class QuestTracker : MonoBehaviour
{
    [ConfettiCannon(particleCount: 50, burstDuration: 1500f)]
    public int questsCompleted = 10;
}

Animated Attributes/Gamification/[FireworkExplosion]

[FireworkExplosion]

Spawns a radial firework shell burst that sparkles, shimmers, and glitters across the inspector upon completion triggers or clicks.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [FireworkExplosion] transforms button presses, field completions, and state changes into celebratory visual fireworks. It simulates an aerial shell detonation that blooms outward in a circular radius (ExplosionRadius) with glittering particle trails (ParticleCount), styled in radiant primary hues (PrimaryColor) that dissipate gracefully over a defined timeframe (Duration).

The Problem: Highlighting high-value milestones—such as unlocking achievements, beating campaign bosses, or completing asset validation checks—feels underwhelming when only conveyed by static checkboxes or small notification text.

The Solution: Decorating critical triggers and methods with [FireworkExplosion]:

  • Radiant Shell Bloom: Launches a 360-degree fireworks burst with randomized trail velocities and twinkle decay.
  • Customizable Color Scheme: Pick signature celebratory colors (PrimaryColor), such as hot magenta, neon gold, or electric cyan.
  • Adjustable Blast Radius: Scale burst spread (ExplosionRadius) to fit compact inspector panels or expansive inspector headers.
  • Multi-Target Support: Attach to fields, properties, or clickable methods ([Button]) to celebrate automated or manual actions.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using System.Collections.Generic;
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.Runtime;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

namespace MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes.Sample
{
    [AddComponentMenu("Maharaja Studio/MSUltimate/Samples/Animated Attributes/Gamification/Firework Explosion Example")]
    public class FireworkExplosionExample : ModularMonoBehaviour
    {
        // ─── 1. Standalone Fields ────────────────────────────────────────────────
        [BoxGroup("1. Standalone Firework Shell Bursts", BackgroundColor = "#1F0718", BorderColor = "#DB2777", BorderRadius = 6, MarginBottom = 8)]
        [FireworkExplosion(primaryColor: "#EC4899", radius: 75f)]
        public string bossDefeatedAchievement = "DRAGON SLAYER (Unlocked)";

        [FireworkExplosion(primaryColor: "#F59E0B", radius: 80f)]
        public int playerMilestoneLevel = 50;

        // ─── 2. Inside Class Demonstration ───────────────────────────────────────
        [BoxGroup("2. Inside Class Demonstration", BackgroundColor = "#26091E", BorderColor = "#BE185D", BorderRadius = 6, MarginBottom = 8)]
        public FestivalRocketSlot finaleRocketSlot = new FestivalRocketSlot();

        [System.Serializable]
        public class FestivalRocketSlot
        {
            public string rocketName = "Supernova Comet Shell";
            [FireworkExplosion(primaryColor: "#A855F7", radius: 70f)]
            public int shellCaliberMm = 120;
        }

        // ─── 3. Direct Collections Cascading & Batch Action ──────────────────────
        [BoxGroup("3. Direct Collections Cascading & Batch Action", BackgroundColor = "#170512", BorderColor = "#9D174D", BorderRadius = 6, MarginBottom = 8)]
        [InfoBox("Direct collection cascading: [FireworkExplosion] implements ICollectionElementAttribute to spawn localized firework bursts on individual array/list rows, and ICollectionBatchAttribute to surface a '🎆' batch celebration header button.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]

        [FireworkExplosion(primaryColor: "#A855F7", radius: 70f)]
        public int[] shellSalvoCalibersMm = new int[] { 75, 100, 125, 150 };

        [FireworkExplosion(primaryColor: "#38BDF8", radius: 65f)]
        public List<string> celebratoryStageCues = new List<string>
        {
            "Opener: Crimson Peony",
            "Mid-Show: Golden Willow Cascade",
            "Finale: Titanium Salvo Burst"
        };

        // ─── 4. Interactive Playground ───────────────────────────────────────────
        [BoxGroup("4. Pyrotechnic Launch Controls", BackgroundColor = "#2D0C24", BorderColor = "#F472B6", BorderRadius = 6)]
        [FireworkExplosion(primaryColor: "#38BDF8", radius: 90f)]
        [Button("Launch Grand Finale Firework! (Cyan 90px)", BackgroundColor = "#DB2777", TextColor = "#FFFFFF")]
        public void LaunchGrandFinale()
        {
            Debug.Log("[FireworkExplosion] WHOOSH... BOOM! Grand finale firework shell detonated.");
        }
    }
}

Animated Attributes/Gamification/[FloatUpDeltaNumber]

[FloatUpDeltaNumber]

Spawns floating RPG combat-style delta text (+/- change) that drifts upward and fades out whenever a numeric property mutates.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [FloatUpDeltaNumber] introduces floating damage and healing numbers directly into the Unity Inspector. Whenever the monitored numeric field or property changes, the attribute calculates the mathematical difference (Δ), creates an animated floating number badge formatted as +N or -N, and floats it upward (FloatDistance) while fading over a smooth curve (Duration).

The Problem: While adjusting combat damage, experience point gains, gold economies, or stamina curves in the inspector, it is difficult to perceive the exact delta between the old value and the newly entered or modified value. Designers frequently lose track of how much an attribute was bumped.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields with [FloatUpDeltaNumber]:

  • Instant Delta Readout: Automatically displays the signed arithmetic difference (+15, -500) the microsecond a change occurs.
  • Color-Coded Semantic Polarity: Distinguishes gains and losses with distinct customizable tints (PositiveColor and NegativeColor).
  • Smooth RPG Drift Animation: Floats upward smoothly by a configurable pixel distance (FloatDistance) before dissolving.
  • Zero Editor Latency: Built on top of UI Toolkit transient visual elements, bypassing IMGUI layout recalculations and garbage generation.

2. Quick Start

csharp

{
    [FloatUpDeltaNumber(positiveColor: "#10B981", negativeColor: "#EF4444")]
    public int goldCoins = 1500;
}

Animated Attributes/Gamification/[LevelUpSparkle]

[LevelUpSparkle]

Spawns glittering golden stars and sparkles that drift upward whenever a property or field value increases.

Package: MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes Targets: Field | Property AllowMultiple: False Inherited: True Performance: High (GPU Painter2D + MeshGenerationContext)

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [LevelUpSparkle] delivers gratifying RPG progression feedback inside the Unity Inspector. When the assigned field or property is modified and its numeric value increases, the attribute spawns a cluster of glittering golden micro-stars (StarCount) in a custom color (SparkleColor) that shimmer and drift upward (FloatDistance) over an animated duration (Duration).

The Problem: Progression values—such as character levels, skill ranks, mastery points, or enhancement tiers—should feel rewarding when upgraded during playtesting or design tuning. In the default inspector, increasing a character level from 1 to 50 provides identical sensory feedback to modifying an arbitrary debug index.

The Solution: Decorating growth metrics with [LevelUpSparkle]:

  • Unidirectional Positive Trigger: Only triggers when the value actually increases, remaining silent during resets or decreases.
  • Glittering Micro-Particle Trail: Radiates twinkling 4-point stars that float upward and spin gently, mirroring classic JRPG leveling sequences.
  • Direct Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute to cascade upward golden sparkle bursts to individual collection element rows in numeric arrays and lists.
  • Customizable Star Canopy: Adjust the number of stars (StarCount), their upward climb distance (FloatDistance), and color scheme (SparkleColor).
  • Clean UI Lifecycle: Automatically recycles and disposes UI elements without adding allocations to your editor loop.

2. Quick Start

csharp

public class HeroProgress : MonoBehaviour

5. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.Runtime;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

namespace MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes.Sample
{
    public class LevelUpSparkleExample : ModularMonoBehaviour
    {
        [Title("LevelUpSparkle Attribute Showcase", "Twinkling golden star particle physics drifting upward on progression")]
        [InfoBox("LevelUpSparkle spawns glittering starburst particles that drift upward and twinkle whenever an integer or float value increases.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]

        [BoxGroup("1. Standalone Progression Sparkles")]
        [LevelUpSparkle(starCount: 16, sparkleColor: "#FBBF24")]
        public int characterLevel = 45;

        [LevelUpSparkle(starCount: 14, sparkleColor: "#38BDF8")]
        public int weaponRefinementTier = 7;

        [BoxGroup("2. Inside Class Demonstration")]
        public HeroStatNode primaryAttribute = new HeroStatNode();

        [BoxGroup("3. Character Core Stat Manifest")]
        [InfoBox("Direct numeric collections decorated with [LevelUpSparkle] automatically cascade star bursts to individual elements when edited.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]
        [LevelUpSparkle(starCount: 14, sparkleColor: "#FBBF24")]
        public int[] heroSkillRanks = new int[] { 10, 25, 40, 55 };

        public List<HeroStatNode> companionStats = new List<HeroStatNode>
        {
            new HeroStatNode { attributeName = "Vitality & Resilience", statRankPoints = 42 },
            new HeroStatNode { attributeName = "Arcane Intelligence", statRankPoints = 50 }
        };

        [Serializable]
        public class HeroStatNode
        {
            public string attributeName = "Agility & Reflexes";

            [LevelUpSparkle(starCount: 15, sparkleColor: "#10B981")]
            public int statRankPoints = 35;
        }

        [BoxGroup("4. Character Progression Controls")]
        [Button("Level Up Hero! (+1 Level)", BackgroundColor = "#F59E0B", TextColor = "#1C1003")]
        public void LevelUpHero()
        {
            characterLevel++;
            Debug.Log($"<color=#FBBF24>[LevelUpSparkle]</color> LEVEL UP! Hero reached Level {characterLevel}.");
        }
    }
}

Animated Attributes/Particle Simulations/[ElectricArcSparks]

[ElectricArcSparks]

Renders procedural branching lightning bolts and high-voltage sparks arcing across field borders on interaction or validation events.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [ElectricArcSparks] electrifies the inspector with procedural electric arcs and branching lightning forks. It generates dynamic lightning paths along the perimeter of the field using randomized fractal subdivision, featuring customizable branching likelihood (BranchChance), strike pacing intervals (ArcInterval), and vivid neon arc coloration (ArcColor).

The Problem: High-energy game mechanics—such as lightning magic, power grid status, cybernetic overloads, or shields—appear static and uninspired when represented as simple numeric fields or standard sliders. Designers lack an immediate sensory cue that a property represents high voltage or unstable electrical power.

The Solution: Decorating electric or energetic properties with [ElectricArcSparks]:

  • Procedural Fractal Lightning: Simulates authentic branching electrical discharge across inspector element borders.
  • Controlled Strike Cadence: Tune strike frequency (ArcInterval) from persistent high-voltage hums to intermittent static discharges.
  • Branching Fork Dynamics: Adjust the probability (BranchChance) that secondary forks splinter off primary lightning channels.
  • Universal Decorator Binding: Attach to fields, properties, or action buttons ([Button]) to simulate charging or firing high-energy weapons.

[FloatingBubbles]

Spawns gentle rising soap bubbles that wobble with fluid buoyancy and pop into tiny droplets upon cursor hover or interaction.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [FloatingBubbles] imbues inspector fields with relaxing, organic ambiance by rendering buoyant translucent bubbles. When the developer moves their mouse cursor over an active bubble, it wobbles elastically and pops into mist droplets.

The Problem: Aquatic gameplay parameters, potion brewing mechanics, underwater vehicle stats, or playful casual games often look clinical when presented in standard dark-grey inspector palettes. There is no atmospheric ambient movement to convey fluid immersion or lighthearted design.

The Solution: Decorating fluid, aquatic, or potion variables with [FloatingBubbles]:

  • Interactive Cursor Popping: Moving your mouse over active bubbles triggers a playful wobble-and-pop particle splash.
  • Buoyancy & Fluid Dynamics: Simulates authentic micro-wobble and sinusoidal lateral drift as bubbles rise through the container.
  • Atmospheric Visual Polish: Adjust bubble count (BubbleCount), upward buoyancy velocity (RiseSpeed), and iridescent color tint (BubbleTint).
  • GPU-Friendly Drawing: Employs low-cost vector primitives that sleep when the inspector tab is defocused.

Animated Attributes/Particle Simulations/[ParticleSnowfall]

[ParticleSnowfall]

Spawns falling weather particles with lateral wind drift and gentle oscillation across the field or container background.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [ParticleSnowfall] introduces serene winter weather aesthetics to the Unity Inspector. It generates a continuous cascade of falling snowflakes (ParticleCount) drifting downward at a customizable velocity (FallSpeed), while applying sinusoidal lateral wind turbulence (WindSway) and customized snowflake pigmentation (ParticleColor).

The Problem: Winter environments, alpine level configurations, holiday event content, or ice magic properties often blend into standard inspector containers without conveying a frosty or wintry theme. Designers tweaking winter balance lack visual thematic context.

The Solution: Decorating winter, blizzard, or ice variables with [ParticleSnowfall]:

  • Organic Wind Turbulence: Snowflakes sway side-to-side dynamically with harmonic wind oscillation (WindSway).
  • Configurable Weather Intensity: Scale from light flurries (ParticleCount = 15) to blizzard cascades (ParticleCount = 60) with faster falling velocity (FallSpeed).
  • Color Customization: Render pristine white snow ("#FFFFFF"), ethereal icy cyan ("#E0F2FE"), or ash fall for volcanic winter settings.
  • Seamless Recycling: Snowflakes loop infinitely by wrapping from bottom to top bounds, maintaining steady frame rates without garbage collection.

Animated Attributes/Particle Simulations/[VaporizeDisintegrate]

[VaporizeDisintegrate]

Spawns a disintegration particle dissolve effect when a field is reset, cleared, modified, or executed via button.

Package: MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes Targets: Field | Property | Method AllowMultiple: False Inherited: True Performance: High (Hardware-Accelerated UI Toolkit 2D Particle Meshes)

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [VaporizeDisintegrate] delivers a sci-fi or dark fantasy dissolution effect inside the Unity Inspector. When an attached field or collection element row is modified, cleared, or an action method is triggered, the element dissolves into a cloud of disintegrating ash or ember particles (ParticleCount) that float away and fade over a designated duration (Duration) in a chosen vaporize tint (DisintegrateColor).

The Problem: Destructive or irreversible editor operations—such as clearing save game caches, deleting local high scores, resetting tuning profiles to defaults, or purging unlinked assets—often execute abruptly without visual weight. Designers can inadvertently clear data without a clear visual consequence.

The Solution: Decorating destructive fields, reset properties, or collection items with [VaporizeDisintegrate]:

  • Visceral Dissolution FX: Renders high-tech digital de-rezzing or burning ash disintegration directly across the field frame.
  • Collection Element Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute so clearing, modifying, or deleting individual items in collections (float[], List<T>) triggers ash dissolution on that exact row.
  • Visual Impact for Resets: Gives clear, satisfying visual confirmation when purging, clearing, or overwriting critical data.
  • Customizable Disintegration Color: Style dissolution particles as glowing amber embers ("#F59E0B"), digital plasma cyan ("#06B6D4"), or void purple ("#9333EA").
  • Universal Decorator Support: Easily attaches to fields, properties, or clickable buttons ([Button]).

2. Quick Start

5. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.Runtime;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

namespace MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes.Sample
{
    public class VaporizeDisintegrateExample : ModularMonoBehaviour
    {
        [Title("VaporizeDisintegrate Attribute Showcase", "Disintegration particle dissolve physics")]
        [InfoBox("VaporizeDisintegrate spawns animated disintegration particles that dissolve into ash and vapor.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]

        [BoxGroup("1. Standalone Disintegration Fields")]
        [VaporizeDisintegrate(count: 45, duration: 700f, DisintegrateColor = "#F59E0B")]
        public string contrabandItemTag = "Unstable Dark Matter Core";

        [VaporizeDisintegrate(count: 35, duration: 500f, DisintegrateColor = "#EF4444")]
        public float incineratorCoreCelsius = 1850f;

        [BoxGroup("2. Inside Class Demonstration")]
        public WasteDisposalBay primaryIncineratorBay = new WasteDisposalBay();

        [BoxGroup("3. Facility Incineration Matrix")]
        [InfoBox("VaporizeDisintegrate cascades down to collections, spawning disintegration ash particles per element row.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]
        [VaporizeDisintegrate(count: 30, duration: 500f, DisintegrateColor = "#F59E0B")]
        public float[] fuelCanisterResidues = new float[] { 45.5f, 80.0f, 12.5f, 95.0f };

        public List<WasteDisposalBay> disposalChambers = new List<WasteDisposalBay>
        {
            new WasteDisposalBay { bayIdentifier = "Slag Recycler Chamber A", payloadMassKg = 150.0f },
            new WasteDisposalBay { bayIdentifier = "Bio-Ash Smelter Chamber B", payloadMassKg = 210.0f }
        };

        [Serializable]
        public class WasteDisposalBay
        {
            public string bayIdentifier = "Hazardous Plasma Crucible 07";

            [VaporizeDisintegrate(count: 40, duration: 650f, DisintegrateColor = "#F97316")]
            public float payloadMassKg = 320.0f;
        }

        [BoxGroup("4. Disintegration Beam Controls")]
        [VaporizeDisintegrate(count: 65, duration: 900f, DisintegrateColor = "#EF4444")]
        [Button("Vaporize All Contraband! (Incinerate)", BackgroundColor = "#EF4444", TextColor = "#FFFFFF")]
        public void VaporizeContraband()
        {
            contrabandItemTag = "<DISINTEGRATED INTO ASH>";
            incineratorCoreCelsius = 3200f;
            primaryIncineratorBay.payloadMassKg = 0f;
            Debug.LogWarning("<color=#F87171>[VaporizeDisintegrate]</color> DISINTEGRATION COMPLETE! Ash particles vaporized.");
        }
    }
}

Animated Attributes/Physics & Interactions/[CoinFlipToggle]

[CoinFlipToggle]

Renders a boolean field as a flipping 3D coin with customizable heads/tails icons, physical spin animation, and collection cascading.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [CoinFlipToggle] replaces standard flat boolean checkboxes with an engaging 3D flipping coin token. When clicked, the coin flips along its horizontal axis with simulated rotational inertia over a configurable duration (FlipDuration), transitioning smoothly between its "heads" (true) state (TrueColor, TrueIcon) and "tails" (false) state (FalseColor, FalseIcon). It fully supports collection cascading via ICollectionElementAttribute.

The Problem: Standard Unity boolean toggles are diminutive checkboxes that offer little visual flair or physical satisfaction. When toggling critical game state flags—such as PVP enabled, hardcore mode active, permadeath, or faction alignment—a plain checkbox lacks presence.

The Solution: Decorating boolean fields with [CoinFlipToggle]:

  • Physical 3D Coin Rotation: Simulates tactile rotational perspective and metallic shading during flips.
  • Custom Glyphs & Color Schemes: Pick distinct icons (e.g., ★, ✕, ⚔, 🛡) and rich hex colors for both true and false faces.
  • Interactive or Read-Only: Enable full interactive click flipping (IsEditable = true) or use as animated telemetry indicators.
  • Full Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute to effortlessly render lists and arrays of booleans as rows of flipping coins.

{
    [CoinFlipToggle(trueColor: "#F59E0B", falseColor: "#64748B", trueIcon: "★", falseIcon: "✕")]

Animated Attributes/Physics & Interactions/[ElasticSnapSlider]

[ElasticSnapSlider]

Renders a slider with magnetic snap notches and elastic spring bounce when snapping to step intervals, with dynamic bounds and collection support.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [ElasticSnapSlider] elevates standard numeric sliders into tactile, magnetic controls. As the user scrubs the slider, the thumb handle exhibits magnetic pull toward defined step intervals (SnapStep). When entering the magnetic zone (SnapThreshold), the handle snaps firmly into place with a subtle harmonic spring bounce. It supports dynamic bounds, companion numeric field input, and collection cascading.

The Problem: Standard Unity sliders ([Range]) move linearly without tactile stops. When tuning discrete intervals—such as audio volume increments (0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%), grid tile subdivisions, or weapons fire rates—designers frequently struggle to hit exact target increments using mouse drags.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields with [ElasticSnapSlider]:

  • Magnetic Step Snapping: Pulls the slider handle magnetically to specified step intervals (SnapStep) within range (SnapThreshold).
  • Tactile Elastic Rebound: Produces an elastic spring overshoot and settle effect upon snapping.
  • Dynamic Limits Binding: Bind maximums dynamically via dynamicMaxValueMember or DynamicMinValueMember.
  • Integrated Precision Input Field: Includes a companion numeric text field (DrawInputField = true) for direct manual typing.
  • Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute for arrays and lists of numeric values.

2. Quick Start

csharp

}
