Animated Attributes/Physics & Interactions/[HoldToConfirmButton]

[HoldToConfirmButton]

Converts an inspector action button or method into a press-and-hold confirmation control with radial/linear progress fill and warning rumbles.

Package: MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes Targets: Method | Field | Property AllowMultiple: False Inherited: True Performance: High (UI Toolkit Pointer Events & Transitions)

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HoldToConfirmButton] safeguards dangerous or irreversible operations by requiring the user to click and continuously hold down the mouse button for a designated timeframe (HoldDurationSeconds). During the hold gesture, an animated progress fill advances (FillColor). If released before reaching 100%, the action aborts and the progress quickly rewinds. Approaching completion triggers an escalating visual rumble pulse (RumbleOnApproach).

The Problem: Destructive buttons in custom inspectors—such as "Delete All Save Data", "Reset Player Profile", "Reformat Level Geometry", or "Purge Texture Atlas"—can easily be triggered by accidental misclicks. Confirmation popup dialogs (EditorUtility.DisplayDialog) disrupt developer flow and quickly become reflexively dismissed.

The Solution: Decorating critical action methods or fields with [HoldToConfirmButton]:

  • Physical Hold Confirmation: Requires conscious deliberate action by holding the mouse down for a set interval (e.g. 1.5 seconds).
  • Smooth Dynamic Progress Fill: Renders an animated progress fill (FillColor) that winds down instantly if released early.
  • Collection Row Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute to safeguard destructive item deletion or reset actions across array and list rows.
  • Tension-Building Rumble FX: Shakes and pulses gently as the hold approaches 100% completion (RumbleOnApproach = true).
  • Flexible Button Typography: Customize button label text (ButtonText) to convey action purpose directly.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class GameDataMaintenance : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HoldToConfirmButton(holdSeconds: 2.0f, buttonText: "Purge All Cloud Saves")]
    public void DeleteAllCloudSaves()
    {
        Debug.Log("Cloud saves permanently removed!");
    }
}

csharp
using System;

using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

namespace MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes.Sample
{
    public class HoldToConfirmButtonExample : ModularMonoBehaviour

        [Title("HoldToConfirmButton Attribute Showcase", "Continuous press duration gate with progress fill")]
        [InfoBox("HoldToConfirmButton safeguards catastrophic actions by demanding a sustained mouse hold.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]

        [BoxGroup("1. Critical Safeguarded Actions")]
        [HoldToConfirmButton(holdSeconds: 2.0f, buttonText: "Hold to Format Cloud Save", FillColor = "#EF4444", RumbleOnApproach = true)]
        public void FormatCloudSave()
        {
            saveProfileIntegrity = 0;
            Debug.LogWarning("<color=#EF4444>[HoldToConfirmButton]</color> CONFIRMED: Cloud save formatted to factory state!");
        }

        public TerminalSecuritySlot commandTerminal = new TerminalSecuritySlot();

        [BoxGroup("3. Security Station Manifest")]
        [InfoBox("HoldToConfirmButton cascades to collection slots, requiring hold-press confirmation before toggling guarded switches.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]
        [HoldToConfirmButton(holdSeconds: 1.0f, buttonText: "Hold to Arm Silo", FillColor = "#EF4444")]
        public bool[] siloArmingSwitches = new bool[] { false, false, true };

        public List<TerminalSecuritySlot> activeSilos = new List<TerminalSecuritySlot>
        {
            new TerminalSecuritySlot { terminalId = "ALPHA-SILO-01", clearanceLevel = 4, isArmingKeyInserted = false },
            new TerminalSecuritySlot { terminalId = "BRAVO-SILO-02", clearanceLevel = 5, isArmingKeyInserted = true }
        };

        {

            public int clearanceLevel = 5;
            public bool isArmingKeyInserted = true;
        }

        [BoxGroup("4. Interactive System Controls")]
        public int saveProfileIntegrity = 100;

        [Button("Simulate Save Data Recovery", BackgroundColor = "#10B981", TextColor = "#022C22")]
        public void RestoreSaveData()
        {
            saveProfileIntegrity = 100;
            Debug.Log("<color=#10B981>[HoldToConfirmButton]</color> Save profile integrity restored to 100%.");
        }
    }
}

Animated Attributes/Physics & Interactions/[LiquidDragSlider]

[LiquidDragSlider]

Deforms the slider thumb into a liquid teardrop in the drag direction with viscous surface tension, snapping back into a droplet on release.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [LiquidDragSlider] delivers fluid, organic tactile feedback for numeric values in the Unity Inspector. When scrubbing the slider rapidly, the circular thumb handle deforms and stretches along the drag vector into an elongated liquid teardrop up to a maximum stretch factor (MaxStretch). Upon releasing the mouse, surface tension snaps the teardrop back into a resting droplet with a smooth fluid wobble. It supports dynamic bounds, companion numeric typing, and collection cascading.

The Problem: Most sliders in developer tools look like rigid geometric widgets. When tuning organic, viscous, or fluid systems—such as slime elasticity, water volume, potion viscosity, fuel reserves, or paint density—rigid UI handles fail to evoke the physical property being configured.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields with [LiquidDragSlider]:

  • Liquid Teardrop Deformation: Dynamically deforms the handle proportional to drag velocity and direction.
  • Surface Tension Snapping: Springs back into a resting sphere with subtle fluid damping upon cursor release.
  • Dynamic Capacity Limits: Bind maximum limits dynamically via dynamicMaxValueMember or DynamicMinValueMember.
  • Integrated Precision Input Field: Includes a companion numeric text field (DrawInputField = true) for direct manual entry.
  • Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute for lists and arrays of fluid parameters.

Animated Attributes/Physics & Interactions/[SpringyBounce]

[SpringyBounce]

Applies damped harmonic oscillator spring physics on click, hover, or value change events across inspector fields.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [SpringyBounce] applies organic squash-and-stretch spring dynamics to inspector elements. Powered by a damped harmonic oscillator model with customizable spring stiffness (k, Stiffness) and friction damping (c, Damping), the element reacts with a tactile scale impulse (TriggerScaleBump) whenever clicked, hovered, or modified, bouncing and settling realistically back to rest.

The Problem: Standard editor controls are static and visually stiff. Clicking a button, toggling an option, or entering a new value produces zero physical response, creating an interface that feels lifeless and unresponsive compared to modern desktop operating systems and games.

The Solution: Decorating interactive fields and controls with [SpringyBounce]:

  • Damped Harmonic Physics: Simulates true Hooke's law spring oscillation with second-order differential dynamics.
  • Micro-Scale Impulse: Triggers a momentary scale punch (TriggerScaleBump), stretching and compressing the UI frame before settling.
  • Tuning Versatility: Adjust spring stiffness (Stiffness) and damping ratio (Damping) to achieve anything from stiff mechanical clicks to jelly-like wobbles.
  • Universal Field Compatibility: Decorate fields or properties to breathe life into everyday data entry.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class BouncyUI : MonoBehaviour
{
    [SpringyBounce(stiffness: 180f, damping: 14f)]
    public float bouncyTension = 1.0f;
}

Animated Attributes/Physics & Interactions/[Tilt3DHover]

[Tilt3DHover]

Tilts the inspector element in pseudo-3D perspective toward the cursor with dynamic specular lighting glare on mouse movement.

Package: MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes Targets: Field | Property AllowMultiple: False Inherited: True Performance: High (Hardware-Accelerated UI Toolkit 3D Transforms)

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [Tilt3DHover] replicates modern interactive holographic card ergonomics inside the Unity Inspector. As the mouse cursor hovers across the decorated field, card, or collection element row, the element tilts smoothly in 3D perspective along the X and Y axes up to a configurable maximum tilt angle (MaxTiltAngle). Simultaneously, a subtle specular glare gradient moves dynamically across the surface (ShowSpecularGlare = true), creating a tangible illusion of depth.

The Problem: Standard 2D flat editor layouts lack visual hierarchy and feel static. Important hero cards, primary configuration panels, or inventory item profiles look identical to ordinary raw data fields.

The Solution: Decorating hero fields or collection cards with [Tilt3DHover]:

  • Parallax 3D Card Tilt: Calculates mouse offset from element center to calculate realistic yaw and pitch rotations.
  • Collection Element Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute so trading cards and collection rows tilt independently under the user's cursor.
  • Dynamic Specular Light Glare: Moves a glossy light reflection across the surface to mimic physical acrylic or trading cards.
  • Configurable Perspective: Control depth intensity (PerspectiveDepth) and maximum tilt rotation (MaxTiltAngle).
  • Smooth Recovery Tweening: Gently springs back to flat alignment as soon as the mouse exits the element rect.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class InventoryItemCard : MonoBehaviour
{
    [Tilt3DHover(maxTilt: 10f)]
    public string itemLoreDescription = "Ancient Dragon Scale";
}

Animated Attributes/Productivity/[BenchmarkFPSMeter]

[BenchmarkFPSMeter]

Renders a live micro-speedometer gauge and rolling graph displaying real-time FPS and frame time directly inside the inspector.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [BenchmarkFPSMeter] embeds an active performance telemetry gauge into the inspector. It renders a circular analog speedometer needle alongside real-time framerate and frame-time measurements (ms), calibrated against a customizable baseline target framerate (TargetFPS). The gauge size (GaugeSize) can be scaled, and the target baseline can be interactively scrubbed (IsEditable = true). It also implements ICollectionElementAttribute for multi-metric profiling collections.

The Problem: Profiling scene optimizations, physics load, procedural meshing scripts, or particle bottlenecks usually forces developers to juggle external Profiler windows or Game view statistics overlays. This breaks workflow focus when tweaking performance-heavy numbers in the Inspector.

The Solution: Decorating performance-critical fields or profile targets with [BenchmarkFPSMeter]:

  • Integrated Live FPS Readout: Continuously samples rendering framerate and draws a dynamic analog speedometer gauge directly above or beside the property.
  • Dynamic Threshold Coloration: Changes color automatically from green (above target) to amber (marginal) to red (dropping below TargetFPS).
  • Configurable Gauge Dimensions: Scale the speedometer diameter (GaugeSize) from a compact 40px chip to a prominent 80px instrument.
  • Direct Input Companion Support: Keeps the underlying serialized property editable (DrawInputField = true) for manual input adjustments.

2. Quick Start

using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

    public float targetFrameRate = 60f;

Animated Attributes/Productivity/[CooldownProgressRing]

[CooldownProgressRing]

Renders a circular progress ring that visually winds down as a cooldown timer ticks, with dynamic duration bindings and collection support.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [CooldownProgressRing] replaces standard linear progress bars with an elegant radial cooldown indicator. As a cooldown value counts down or recharges, the circular arc sweeps smoothly from 0 to 360 degrees, rendered in customizable foreground (RingColor) and background (BackgroundColor) colors. The diameter (RingSize) is fully adjustable, dynamic upper durations can be bound via reflection (DynamicMaxDurationMember), and circular dragging allows interactive scrubbing.

The Problem: Linear bars require significant horizontal layout width and look bulky when grouped beside skill icons, spell matrices, or weapon loadouts. Displaying dozens of cooldown timers in compact inspector cards causes clutter.

The Solution: Decorating timer fields with [CooldownProgressRing]:

  • Radial Cooldown Arc: Draws a compact circular ring that winds clockwise or counter-clockwise as cooldowns expire.
  • Dynamic Capacity Binding: Binds maximum cooldown duration dynamically via dynamicMaxDurationMember.
  • Flexible Numeric Overlays: Toggle numeric time text (ShowValue) or percentage readouts (ShowPercentage).
  • Interactive Radial Scrubbing: Click or drag around the circle (IsEditable = true) to scrub cooldown seconds in real time.
  • Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute for lists and arrays of ability cooldown timers.

2. Quick Start

Animated Attributes/Productivity/[DiffHighlightFlash]

[DiffHighlightFlash]

Displays a breathing neon outline highlight whenever a field value differs from its initial or prefab state, aiding version control reviews.

Package: MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes Targets: Field | Property AllowMultiple: False Inherited: True Performance: High (Hardware Accelerated UI Toolkit Border Transitions)

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [DiffHighlightFlash] provides instantaneous visual diff tracking inside the Unity Inspector. As soon as a serialized property's value deviates from its initial baseline (or prefab parent value), the attribute wraps the field in an animated, breathing outline glow (DiffColor) oscillating with configurable intensity (PulseIntensity). When reverted back to baseline, the highlight gracefully extinguishes.

The Problem: During scene setup, balance passes, or prefab editing, developers often tweak dozens of properties. Before saving or committing, it is easy to forget which subtle sliders, toggles, or numbers were changed versus which ones remained at default values, leading to unintended git commits or forgotten test values.

The Solution: Decorating mutable inspector properties with [DiffHighlightFlash]:

  • Unsaved Modification Radar: Immediately flags any modified property with an eye-catching breathing border.
  • Collection Row Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute to pinpoint the exact dirty or modified element row inside large arrays and tables.
  • Color-Coded Status Accent: Choose custom diff outline colors (DiffColor), such as warm amber ("#F59E0B"), electric cyan, or git purple.
  • Configurable Pulse Breath: Adjust the oscillation intensity (PulseIntensity) from a gentle ambient glow to an urgent flashing reminder.
  • Effortless Prefab & Git Audits: Glance at complex inspector panels and instantly spot every altered value before committing.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class LevelTuningProfile : MonoBehaviour
{
    [DiffHighlightFlash(diffColor: "#F59E0B")]
    public float enemySpawnIntervalSeconds = 3.5f;
}

5. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.Runtime;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

namespace MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes.Sample
{
    public class DiffHighlightFlashExample : ModularMonoBehaviour
    {
        [Title("DiffHighlightFlash Attribute Showcase", "Visual dirty-state diff pulsing")]
        [InfoBox("DiffHighlightFlash projects a subtle breathing outline pulse onto any field whose value differs from its baseline.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]

        [BoxGroup("1. Standalone Diff Trackers")]
        [DiffHighlightFlash(diffColor: "#F59E0B", PulseIntensity = 1.0f)]
        public string characterClassArchetype = "Shadow Assassin";

        [DiffHighlightFlash(diffColor: "#10B981", PulseIntensity = 1.2f)]
        public float baseAgilityScore = 48.5f;

        [BoxGroup("2. Inside Class Demonstration")]
        public CombatTuningNode combatParameters = new CombatTuningNode();

        [BoxGroup("3. Server Configuration Tuning Manifest")]
        [InfoBox("Direct float[] arrays decorated with [DiffHighlightFlash] display individual amber breathing diff highlights on modified elements.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]
        [DiffHighlightFlash(diffColor: "#F59E0B")]
        public float[] balancingStatMultipliers = new float[] { 1.0f, 1.25f, 1.5f, 2.0f };

        public List<CombatTuningNode> combatTuningProfiles = new List<CombatTuningNode>
        {
            new CombatTuningNode { damageFormula = "Physical Melee Standard", criticalDamageMultiplier = 2.0f },
            new CombatTuningNode { damageFormula = "Spell Magic Pierce", criticalDamageMultiplier = 1.75f }
        };

        [Serializable]
        public class CombatTuningNode
        {
            public string damageFormula = "BaseAtk * (1.0 + StatBonus)";

            [DiffHighlightFlash(diffColor: "#F43F5E", PulseIntensity = 1.3f)]
            public float criticalDamageMultiplier = 2.5f;
        }

        [BoxGroup("4. Interactive Balance Sandbox")]
        [Button("Tweak Balances (+10% Agility, +0.5x Crit)", BackgroundColor = "#F59E0B", TextColor = "#1C1102")]
        public void TweakBalancingValues()
        {
            baseAgilityScore += 5.0f;
            combatParameters.criticalDamageMultiplier += 0.5f;
            Debug.Log($"<color=#F59E0B>[DiffHighlightFlash]</color> Values tweaked. Diff highlights will illuminate.");
        }
    }
}

Animated Attributes/Productivity/[LiveCollaboratorBeacon]

[LiveCollaboratorBeacon]

Displays a pulsing multiplayer collaborator avatar badge anchored to an inspector field, signaling active editing by a remote teammate.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [LiveCollaboratorBeacon] brings collaborative multiplayer awareness into the Unity Inspector. It anchors a floating, pill-shaped avatar badge (Initials) directly to the top-right corner of the serialized property field (right = -6, top = -6). The badge is rendered in a custom color (AvatarColor) with bold typography, accompanied by a status descriptor (StatusText), and oscillates continuously with a gentle sinusoidal scale pulse (Mathf.Sin(time) * 0.15f + 0.85f) to signal active remote editing or ownership.

The Problem: In multi-developer studios and distributed teams, multiple level designers, gameplay programmers, or technical artists often work on the same scenes, ScriptableObjects, or prefabs simultaneously. Without visual indicators in the editor, team members frequently overwrite each other's serialized property modifications or clash over shared assets.

The Solution: Decorating shared or concurrent properties with [LiveCollaboratorBeacon]:

  • Real-Time Presence Indicators: Instantly shows who is editing or assigned to a specific field via stylized user initials (JD, AL, MS).
  • Pulsing Animation Heartbeat: Draws attention with a low-frequency scale pulse that operates via UI Toolkit scheduling without scene-view lag.
  • Color-Coded Teammates: Assign distinct avatar hex colors (AvatarColor) per developer or department role (e.g. blue for programming, purple for art, orange for design).
  • Non-Destructive Absolute Layout: Anchors as an overlay badge on the target property element without distorting the underlying field width or alignment.

2. Quick Start

Animated Attributes/Productivity/[LiveSyncPulse]

[LiveSyncPulse]

Displays a pulsating live beacon dot and badge indicating active synchronization with runtime or network state, with interactive click toggling.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [LiveSyncPulse] displays a real-time status beacon beside inspector fields. It renders a glowing circular beacon dot (8x8px) that smoothly pulses its alpha transparency according to a configurable cycle rate (PulseRate). Beside the dot is a high-contrast badge (SyncLabel, defaulting to "LIVE"). When interactive mode is enabled (IsEditable = true), clicking the badge triggers an instant Undo-recorded toggle for boolean properties alongside a 1.8x scale pop pulse on the beacon dot. It also implements ICollectionElementAttribute for multi-item list cascading.

The Problem: When developing networked multiplayer games, live telemetry tools, or hot-reloading configurations, developers cannot easily distinguish fields that are synchronized live over socket/IPC connections from static offline properties. Accidental manual edits during runtime can desynchronize states without visual feedback.

The Solution: Decorating live-synced fields with [LiveSyncPulse]:

  • Pulsing Circular Beacon: Uses hardware-accelerated alpha oscillation (PulseRate cycles/sec) to communicate live connectivity.
  • Interactive Click Toggle: Clicking the badge immediately toggles boolean properties with full Unity Undo.RecordObject registration and scale feedback.
  • Companion Field Layout: Supports drawing the standard serialized property field (DrawInputField = true) or displaying standalone beacon labels.
  • Universal Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute to decorate collections, arrays, and lists of telemetry properties.

2. Quick Start

Animated Attributes/Productivity/[SnapshotGhostDiff]

[SnapshotGhostDiff]

Overlays a smooth fading ghost highlight across the field background whenever modifying or undoing property values.

Package: MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes Targets: Field | Property AllowMultiple: False Inherited: True Performance: High (Zero Idle Tick Allocations)

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [SnapshotGhostDiff] tracks serialized property mutations in real time via UI Toolkit's TrackPropertyValue. Whenever the monitored field's value changes or an Undo/Redo operation occurs, the attribute triggers a soft background highlight in a designated tint (GhostColorAdd / GhostColorRemove). The highlight then gracefully interpolates its alpha transparency back to zero over a customizable decay duration (FadeDuration), visually confirming that a mutation took place.

The Problem: When scrubbing sliders, modifying values via hotkeys, or performing bulk Undo/Redo cycles, developers often lose track of which specific field just mutated on a crowded inspector screen. Without transient visual confirmation, it is easy to miss subtle numerical shifts.

The Solution: Decorating monitored fields with [SnapshotGhostDiff]:

  • Instant Change Confirmation: Illuminates the property field background the moment a value delta or Undo action happens.
  • Collection Row Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute to overlay ghost highlights specifically on the mutated element row inside arrays and lists.
  • Graceful Linear Fadeout: Fades out smoothly from 0.4 opacity over a configurable interval (FadeDuration in milliseconds).
  • Customizable Color Accents: Select distinct color tints (GhostColorAdd, GhostColorRemove) to convey increments or decrements.
  • Zero Idle Computation: The drawer registers a transient schedule timer that sleeps completely once the fade progress reaches 100%.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class PlayerInventory : MonoBehaviour
{
    [SnapshotGhostDiff(fadeDuration: 1000f)]
    public int inventoryCapacity = 40;
}

Animated Attributes/Sci-Fi & FX/[ConstellationStarfield]

[ConstellationStarfield]

Renders a cosmic space starfield with drifting, twinkling celestial particles across the inspector field background.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [ConstellationStarfield] turns the background of inspector fields into a deep space viewport. It renders a dark cosmic backdrop (#0A0A14 at 0.85 opacity) filled with procedural stars (StarCount). Powered by UI Toolkit's 2D vector painter (MeshGenerationContext.painter2D), stars drift horizontally across the canvas at a configurable velocity (DriftSpeed), wrapping smoothly at border edges while oscillating their brightness (alpha = (Mathf.Sin(Twinkle) + 1f) * 0.4f + 0.2f) with sinusoidal twinkling.

The Problem: Space simulators, celestial navigation systems, galactic trade economies, or sci-fi procedural generators feel grounded and dull when presented over default dark-grey editor panels. The lack of cosmic ambiance detaches developers from the space-themed game world they are constructing.

The Solution: Decorating interstellar or astronomical properties with [ConstellationStarfield]:

  • Vector-Painted Starfield: Procedurally renders circular stars with randomized sizes (1.0 to 2.5px) and drift speeds using GPU-accelerated UI Toolkit 2D vector arcs.
  • Harmonic Celestial Twinkling: Each star maintains an independent sine wave phase offset for organic, non-uniform twinkling.
  • Continuous Edge Wrapping: Stars wrap seamlessly from right to left borders as they drift, keeping memory allocations at zero.
  • Seamless Field Stacking: Draws behind standard PropertyField inputs, keeping numbers and labels sharp and interactively accessible.

2. Quick Start

csharp

using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

}

[CRTScanlineOverlay]

Overlays retro CRT monitor raster scanlines rolling vertically across the inspector field to evoke vintage arcade and terminal aesthetics.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [CRTScanlineOverlay] applies an authentic retro cathode-ray tube (CRT) monitor effect directly across inspector properties. Using UI Toolkit's 2D vector painter (MeshGenerationContext.painter2D), it draws horizontal raster scanlines with customizable spacing (LineSpacing, defaulting to 4px) and opacity (ScanlineAlpha, defaulting to 0.2). The scanlines roll continuously downward at a designated velocity (ScrollSpeed in px/sec), creating a convincing vintage display aesthetic without obstructing input readability.

The Problem: Retro arcade games, cyberpunk terminals, fallout-style wasteland computers, or analog security monitors often feature rich in-game CRT shaders. However, inside the Unity Editor, data configurations for these systems look modern and generic, breaking the retro immersion during development and tuning.

The Solution: Decorating vintage or terminal parameters with [CRTScanlineOverlay]:

  • Rolling Raster Lines: Continuously scrolls horizontal scanlines downward at a configurable velocity (ScrollSpeed), simulating 60Hz cathode ray scanning.
  • Subtle, Non-Intrusive Alpha: Fine-tune scanline transparency (ScanlineAlpha) and spacing (LineSpacing) so text and numbers remain fully legible.
  • Hardware-Accelerated Vector Strokes: Renders via lightweight painter2D.LineTo paths with zero texture asset dependencies.
  • Non-Blocking Pointer Input: Configured with PickingMode.Ignore, allowing clicks, drags, and text editing to pass straight through to the underlying field.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class ArcadeTerminal : MonoBehaviour
{
    [CRTScanlineOverlay(scrollSpeed: 30f, lineSpacing: 4f)]
    public string terminalBootSequence = "INIT_SYSTEM_V_1984";
}

Animated Attributes/Sci-Fi & FX/[EnergyBorderBeam]

[EnergyBorderBeam]

Renders a high-energy laser beam traveling continuously along the perimeter border of the inspector field.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [EnergyBorderBeam] wraps inspector properties in an animated energy containment field. It renders a subtle base perimeter border (15% opacity), accompanied by an intense, luminous energy beam head (BeamLength, defaulting to 45px) rendered with increased stroke thickness (BorderWidth + 1.5f) in a vibrant neon hue (BeamColor). The beam circulates clockwise along all four edges of the container at an adjustable speed (BeamSpeed), giving the element an active power-line aura.

The Problem: Special gameplay abilities, legendary inventory cards, activated forcefields, or dangerous overclocking options blend in with everyday inspector fields. Static borders fail to convey that an element is currently energized, active, or charged.

The Solution: Decorating high-energy fields with [EnergyBorderBeam]:

  • Continuous Perimeter Circuit: Calculates exact rectangular perimeter coordinates and sweeps a luminous beam seamlessly around corners.
  • Glowing Accent Hue: Custom beam color (BeamColor, defaulting to #38BDF8 sky blue) with configurable stroke width (BorderWidth).
  • Dynamic Speed Tuning: Regulate circulation speed (BeamSpeed) for calm power loops or frantic high-voltage racing beams.
  • Built-In Container Padding: Automatically wraps child fields in generous padding (paddingTop/Bottom = 3, paddingLeft/Right = 4) so borders do not overlap field content.

Animated Attributes/Sci-Fi & FX/[HexGridMatrix]

[HexGridMatrix]

Renders an animated hexagonal honeycomb grid pattern with subtle neon line pulses across the field background.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HexGridMatrix] projects a cyberpunk, high-tech honeycomb tessellation directly into the background of inspector fields. Using UI Toolkit's 2D vector painter (MeshGenerationContext.painter2D), it computes a mathematical hexagonal lattice using trigonometric 6-point polygons (HexRadius, defaulting to 12px) in a translucent neon line style (ColorHex at 12% opacity). The grid updates periodically based on PulseSpeed to convey futuristic energy matrix fields.

The Problem: Nanotechnology parameters, cyberpunk cyberware modules, energy shielding, or tactical turn-based grid maps look mundane against flat monochrome inspector panels. Standard UI layouts do not evoke high-tech hexagonal modularity.

The Solution: Decorating tactical, cybernetic, or shield variables with [HexGridMatrix]:

  • Mathematical Hex Lattice: Generates authentic 6-sided honeycomb cells with accurate spacing (hDist = r * 1.5, vDist = r * sqrt(3)).
  • Subtle Ambient Styling: Rendered at a balanced 12% alpha so input values and labels remain crisp and readable in foreground.
  • Customizable Cell Scale: Scale hexagon cell dimensions (HexRadius) from dense micro-meshes to bold architectural hex plates.
  • Hardware-Accelerated UI Toolkit Paths: Drawn directly via vector strokes with PickingMode.Ignore, adding zero layout lag to your workflow.

Animated Attributes/Sci-Fi & FX/[HologramFlicker]

[HologramFlicker]

Applies holographic transmission interference with periodic micro-flickering, chromatic shifts, and subtle opacity jitter.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [HologramFlicker] gives inspector fields an unstable, ethereal holographic projection aesthetic. Powered by UI Toolkit's scheduling system, it monitors the target property and periodically introduces probabilistic micro-flickering (FlickerIntensity). When a flicker event triggers, the element momentarily jitters its opacity (Random.Range(0.75f, 1.0f)) and tints the text in a futuristic holographic hue (HoloTint), settling back to standard rendering between flicker cycles.

The Problem: Holographic comms arrays, glitchy transmissions, AI avatar dialogues, or damaged telemetry consoles often look too crisp, stable, and solid in the editor. Without subtle transmission instability, they fail to feel like projected light beams traveling across lightyears.

The Solution: Decorating holographic, transmission, or AI properties with [HologramFlicker]:

  • Unstable Projection Jitter: Probabilistically flickers opacity and color based on FlickerIntensity, simulating photon decay.
  • Futuristic Holographic Tint: Applies signature sci-fi projection hues (HoloTint, defaulting to #00F0FF cyan) during micro-flicker events.
  • Zero Distraction Tuning: Designed with subtle ranges (FlickerIntensity = 0.15f) to deliver mood without impeding data entry or causing eye fatigue.
  • Direct Element Modulation: Seamlessly binds to the parent element hierarchy via AnimationTargetHelper, requiring zero extra wrapper containers.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class HologramCommArray : MonoBehaviour
{
    [HologramFlicker(holoTint: "#00F0FF", flickerIntensity: 0.15f)]
    public string incomingTransmissionLog = "RECEIVING ENCRYPTED BEACON...";
}

Animated Attributes/Sci-Fi & FX/[NeonPulseGlow]

[NeonPulseGlow]

Renders a rhythmic breathing neon glow and pulsating border frame around the inspector field container.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [NeonPulseGlow] wraps inspector properties in an illuminated, breathing neon frame. The drawer styles the element with rounded border corners (radius 4px) and continuously modulates the border width between MinSpread (default 1px) and MaxSpread (default 3.5px), while harmonically pulsing the alpha color intensity from 0.3 to 0.85 in a vivid neon tint (GlowColor, defaulting to hot magenta #EC4899). The pulse frequency is calibrated via PulseSpeed in radians per second.

The Problem: Cyberpunk titles, arcade mini-games, neon synthwave themes, or night club management games look out of place when configured using dull default unity controls. Key attributes—such as neon lighting colors, nightclub popularity, or cyberdeck battery life—lack vibrant atmospheric presence.

The Solution: Decorating themed properties with [NeonPulseGlow]:

  • Breathing Border Spread: Dynamically interpolates border stroke thickness between MinSpread and MaxSpread, creating a convincing light bloom.
  • Harmonic Sinusoidal Fading: Calculates alpha glow oscillation smoothly (pulse = (sin(time) + 1) * 0.5) without abrupt cuts.
  • Vibrant Custom Colors: Supports any signature neon hex tone (GlowColor), from neon pink to laser cyan or radioactive lime.
  • Direct Element Integration: Discovered via AnimationTargetHelper, eliminating unnecessary container nesting.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class CyberpunkSign : MonoBehaviour
{
    [NeonPulseGlow(glowColor: "#EC4899", pulseSpeed: 1.5f)]
    public float neonSignLuminance = 1500f;
}

Animated Attributes/Sci-Fi & FX/[ScanningRadar]

[ScanningRadar]

Displays a circular rotating radar screen with targeting crosshairs and a fading phosphor sweep beam beside inspector fields.

Package: MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes Targets: Field | Property AllowMultiple: False Inherited: True Performance: High (Hardware-Accelerated UI Toolkit 2D Vector Meshes)

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [ScanningRadar] embeds a functional 360-degree rotating radar display beside serialized properties and collection items. It renders a circular CRT scope (RadarSize, defaulting to 40px) styled in deep military radar green (#0D1F14). Using UI Toolkit's 2D vector painter, it draws internal crosshairs, a continuous 360-degree radial sweeping line (painter.LineTo), and a fading 45-degree phosphor trail arc (painter.Arc) that rotates continuously according to SweepSpeed (revolutions per second) in a chosen phosphor hue (RadarColor).

The Problem: Submarine navigation, sonar ping systems, tactical radar arrays, or stealth detection radiuses require active visual feedback during balancing. Tweaking detection radiuses, sweep speeds, or target proximity with numeric fields alone provides no visual sense of scanning tempo.

The Solution: Decorating stealth, radar, or detection properties with [ScanningRadar]:

  • Circular Scope Widget: Compact circular instrument placed neatly beside the PropertyField input control.
  • Collection Element Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute so every waypoint or target slot in Vector3[] or List<T> has its own radar sweep indicator.
  • Continuous 360° Sweep: Full rotational sweep line with a trailing 45-degree fading phosphor decay arc.
  • Vector-Drawn Crosshairs: Center reference crosshairs rendered at 25% opacity for authentic military display aesthetics.
  • Configurable Dimensions & Colors: Scale scope diameter (RadarSize) from small indicators to prominent circular gauges in custom phosphor tints (#10B981 green, #38BDF8 cyan, or #F59E0B amber).

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class ScoutDrone : MonoBehaviour
{
    [ScanningRadar(size: 42f, radarColor: "#10B981")]
    public float enemyDetectionRadiusMeters = 75f;
}

5. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.Runtime;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

namespace MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes.Sample
{
    public class ScanningRadarExample : ModularMonoBehaviour
    {
        [Title("ScanningRadar Attribute Showcase", "Continuous 360-degree radar sweep beam")]
        [InfoBox("ScanningRadar renders an authentic circular PPI radar display with a sweeping radial beam.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]

        [BoxGroup("1. Standalone Radar Scanners")]
        [ScanningRadar(size: 45f, radarColor: "#10B981", SweepSpeed = 1.5f)]
        public string primaryAirspaceSector = "SECTOR-BRAVO-AIRSPACE-NORTH";

        [ScanningRadar(size: 38f, radarColor: "#06B6D4", SweepSpeed = 1.0f)]
        public float sonarEchoDepthMeters = 840f;

        [BoxGroup("2. Inside Class Demonstration")]
        public RadarContactNode activeTarget = new RadarContactNode();

        [BoxGroup("3. Monitored Contact Radar Manifest")]
        [InfoBox("ScanningRadar cascades down to collections, rendering individual circular radar sweep displays beside each coordinate row.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]
        [Title("Tactical Airspace Waypoints (Radar Scanline Cascading)")]
        [ScanningRadar(size: 32f, radarColor: "#10B981", SweepSpeed = 1.2f)]
        public Vector3[] airspaceWaypoints = new Vector3[]
        {
            new Vector3(120f, 450f, 30f),
            new Vector3(250f, 480f, 90f),
            new Vector3(380f, 520f, 140f)
        };

        public List<RadarContactNode> contactList = new List<RadarContactNode>
        {
            new RadarContactNode { iffCode = "CIVILIAN-CARGO-77", lockOnDistanceKm = 42.0f },
            new RadarContactNode { iffCode = "ESCORT-FRIGATE-02", lockOnDistanceKm = 8.5f }
        };

        [Serializable]
        public class RadarContactNode
        {
            public string iffCode = "BOGEY-BANDIT-09";

            [ScanningRadar(size: 36f, radarColor: "#F59E0B", SweepSpeed = 1.8f)]
            public float lockOnDistanceKm = 14.8f;
        }

        [BoxGroup("4. Radar Station Controls")]
        [Button("Simulate Unidentified Blip Detection", BackgroundColor = "#10B981", TextColor = "#022C22")]
        public void DetectBlip()
        {
            activeTarget.lockOnDistanceKm = Mathf.Max(1.0f, activeTarget.lockOnDistanceKm - 2.5f);
            Debug.LogWarning($"<color=#34D399>[ScanningRadar]</color> Contact locked! Distance: {activeTarget.lockOnDistanceKm:F1} km.");
        }
    }
}

Context / ArchitectureSupportBehavior & Guidelines

Target Element RolesSupportedCollectionElementRole.Element, Value, NullableValue, TupleItem.
Direct CollectionsSupportedOperates on direct coordinate arrays (Vector3[], Vector2[]), transform lists, and target strings.
Nested Serializable ClassesSupportedFully operates on fields inside child items and structs declared within lists.

Animated Attributes/Sci-Fi & FX/[ShimmerSweep]

[ShimmerSweep]

Sweeps a diagonal metallic light shine beam across the inspector field at periodic intervals to convey luxury and premium rarity.

Package: MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes Targets: Field | Property AllowMultiple: False Inherited: True Performance: High (Hardware-Accelerated UI Toolkit 2D Vector Meshes)

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [ShimmerSweep] casts a periodic metallic sheen across inspector properties and collection element rows. Using UI Toolkit's vector painter (MeshGenerationContext.painter2D), it creates an angled 45-degree translucent parallelogram beam (ShimmerWidth, defaulting to 40px) in a translucent tint (ShimmerColor, defaulting to 40% translucent white). The beam translates smoothly across the entire width of the container from left to right over SweepDuration ms, recurring every Interval seconds.

The Problem: Premium game items—such as legendary loot tiers, rare trading cards, divine artifacts, or VIP memberships—often look indistinguishable from standard common inventory fields in the inspector. Without high-end visual polish, premium items feel ordinary during data authoring.

The Solution: Decorating rare, mythical, or premium assets with [ShimmerSweep]:

  • Periodic Specular Glint: Sweeps an angled metallic shine across the field at defined intervals (Interval in seconds).
  • Collection Element Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute so every item in weapon catalogs or artifact lists receives its own periodic gleam.
  • Customizable Beam Dimensions: Adjust shine beam width (ShimmerWidth) and sweep speed (SweepDuration).
  • Custom Hue & Transparency: Configure gold foil shines (#FBBF2466), celestial silver (#FFFFFF66), or iridescent gemstone tints.
  • Zero-Allocation Translation: Operates via horizontal style offsets (style.left = currentX) within an overflow-hidden wrapper, guaranteeing smooth editor frame rates.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class LegendaryItem : MonoBehaviour
{
    [ShimmerSweep(interval: 3.0f, shimmerColor: "#FBBF2466")]
    public string legendarySwordName = "Excalibur of the Dawn";
}

5. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.Runtime;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

namespace MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes.Sample
{
    public class ShimmerSweepExample : ModularMonoBehaviour
    {
        [Title("ShimmerSweep Attribute Showcase", "Diagonal metallic light sweep gleam")]
        [InfoBox("ShimmerSweep animates an angled specular light shine across the surface of inspector fields at periodic intervals.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]

        [BoxGroup("1. Standalone Metallic Gleams")]
        [ShimmerSweep(interval: 3.5f, shimmerColor: "#FFFFFF66", SweepDuration = 600f, ShimmerWidth = 45f)]
        public string platinumTrophyName = "SOVEREIGN CONQUEROR - PLATINUM 100%";

        [ShimmerSweep(interval: 2.5f, shimmerColor: "#FDE04788", SweepDuration = 500f, ShimmerWidth = 55f)]
        public float weaponCriticalRating = 385.5f;

        [BoxGroup("2. Inside Class Demonstration")]
        public RareLootEntry featuredItem = new RareLootEntry();

        [BoxGroup("3. Treasury Vault Trophy Manifest")]
        [InfoBox("ShimmerSweep cascades down to collections, rendering periodic specular light shines across individual element rows.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]
        [Title("Vault Relic Scepters (Metallic Shimmer Cascading)")]
        [ShimmerSweep(interval: 2.8f, shimmerColor: "#FDE04788", ShimmerWidth = 50f)]

        }

Animated Attributes/Sci-Fi & FX/[SonarPingWave]

[SonarPingWave]

Emits periodic expanding circular sonar rings across the inspector field with a fading phosphor trail to visualize radar pulses.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [SonarPingWave] overlays active acoustic sonar pulses over inspector properties. Using UI Toolkit's 2D vector painter (MeshGenerationContext.painter2D), it emits expanding circular wavefronts (painter.Arc) from the center of the element that expand outward to 60% of the container's width. As the wave propagates, its stroke alpha fades gracefully from 0.6 down to zero over PingDuration ms, recurring periodically at every PingInterval seconds in an acoustic phosphor hue (SonarColor).

The Problem: Underwater exploration games, acoustic stealth mechanics, deep-sea submarine games, or medical ultrasound devices often require tuning pulse frequencies, echo delays, and detection radii. In the editor, adjusting these values feels detached from the wave-propagation physics they represent.

The Solution: Decorating acoustic, sonar, or echolocation variables with [SonarPingWave]:

  • Expanding Circular Wavefront: Generates continuous radial sonar ripples centered across the field.
  • Organic Phosphor Decay: Smoothly fades the stroke intensity as the wave reaches maximum expansion radius.
  • Configurable Pulse Tempo: Fine-tune interval cadence (PingInterval) and wave travel duration (PingDuration).
  • Non-Obtrusive Background Overlay: Rendered with PickingMode.Ignore over an overflow-hidden canvas, ensuring text input remains completely unaffected.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class SubmarineSonar : MonoBehaviour
{
    [SonarPingWave(interval: 2.5f, sonarColor: "#10B981")]
    public float acousticPulseFrequencyHz = 440f;
}

Animated Attributes/Sci-Fi & FX/[TargetLockOnReticle]

[TargetLockOnReticle]

Surrounds target coordinates, references, or combat targets with rotating sci-fi tracking brackets and a center crosshair dot.

Package: MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes Targets: Field | Property AllowMultiple: False Inherited: True Performance: High (GPU Painter2D + MeshGenerationContext)

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [TargetLockOnReticle] equips inspector fields with an active targeting system. It embeds a rotating reticle instrument (ReticleSize, defaulting to 28px) to the left of the property field. Using UI Toolkit's 2D vector painter, it draws 4 rotating corner arc brackets at 90-degree intervals (painter.Arc) and a stationary center targeting dot (painter.Arc(center, 2f, 0, 360)) in a high-visibility tactical hue (ReticleColor, defaulting to #EF4444 red). The brackets spin continuously according to SpinSpeed (revolutions per second).

The Problem: Selecting combat targets, missile lock-on coordinates, homing beacon waypoints, or sniper aim points in the Unity Inspector feels passive when displayed as ordinary Transform references or raw Vector3 coordinate fields.

The Solution: Decorating targeting parameters with [TargetLockOnReticle]:

  • Rotating Tactical Brackets: 4-corner arc brackets continuously spin around the target center, delivering immediate visual lock-on confirmation.
  • Center Targeting Crosshair Dot: Fixed 2px center point anchors the reticle to the target origin.
  • Collection Row Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute to surround each waypoint or missile target in arrays and lists with its own spinning bracket reticle.
  • Configurable Size & Speed: Adjust outer diameter (ReticleSize) and rotation rate (SpinSpeed) from deliberate tracking sweeps to rapid lock-on alerts.
  • Clean Inline Layout: Positioned seamlessly alongside the serialized input field without distorting Inspector indentation.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class MissileGuidance : MonoBehaviour
{
    [TargetLockOnReticle(reticleColor: "#EF4444", spinSpeed: 1.5f)]
    public Transform activeCombatTarget;
}

Animated Attributes/Spatial Controls/[PullToRefreshTrigger]

[PullToRefreshTrigger]

Renders an interactive pull-to-refresh spring gesture that triggers callbacks or methods upon elastic release.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [PullToRefreshTrigger] adapts modern mobile pull-to-refresh UX into the Unity Inspector. It places a tactile pull handle inside a recessed track chamber (#0F172A, height 62px). When dragged vertically with the mouse, it exhibits elastic rubber-band resistance. Dragging beyond the activation threshold (PullThreshold, defaulting to 40px) triggers a vivid threshold bloom (AccentColor, defaulting to #10B981 emerald). Upon mouse release, the handle snaps back with spring kinematics and invokes the target method, callback, or property refresh.

The Problem: Editor maintenance tasks—such as refreshing network databases, querying cloud catalogs, re-scanning asset directories, or refreshing procedural preview caches—are traditionally triggered by ordinary buttons. Buttons can be accidentally clicked, and they lack the deliberate physical intent that prevents accidental server or cache queries.

The Solution: Decorating refresh methods or telemetry properties with [PullToRefreshTrigger]:

  • Tactile Elastic Drag: Simulates true rubber-band resistance, requiring deliberate physical pulling before triggering.
  • Visual Threshold Bloom: Glows brightly with an accent hue (AccentColor) when dragged past the threshold point.
  • Spring Kinematics: Automatically snaps back into resting position upon release with harmonic damping.
  • Method & Property Compatibility: Overrides both property (OnGUI) and method (OnMethodGUI) execution, with full collection cascading via ICollectionElementAttribute.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class AssetCatalogManager : MonoBehaviour
{
    [PullToRefreshTrigger(pullThreshold: 45f, triggerText: "Pull to Sync Catalog")]
    public void RefreshRemoteAssetCatalog()
    {
        Debug.Log("Asset catalog queried and synced!");
    }
}

Animated Attributes/Spatial Controls/[RotaryKnob]

[RotaryKnob]

Replaces numeric fields with an interactive circular rotary dial knob operated via angular drag gestures, with dynamic bounds and magnetic snapping.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [RotaryKnob] transforms standard linear numeric inputs into hardware-style analog rotary dials. Operated via angular circular mouse drags, the dial updates numeric values between MinValue and MaxValue (or dynamic bounds via DynamicMaxValueMember). It renders a visual value arc (ArcColor), directional pointer needle (PointerColor), optional magnetic step snapping (SnapInterval), and configurable numeric readout formatting (ValueFormat). It also implements ICollectionElementAttribute for multi-item list cascading.

The Problem: Audio synthesizers, volume attenuators, radial dials, steer angles, or vintage hardware consoles are intrinsically circular controls. Tuning rotational values using flat horizontal sliders feels counter-intuitive and clutters complex audio/physics panels.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields with [RotaryKnob]:

  • Hardware Dial Ergonomics: Circular mouse dragging around the dial center provides natural rotational torque feedback.
  • Magnetic Snap Intervals: Snap to discrete steps (e.g., 5°, 10°, 45°) via SnapInterval, or set to 0f for smooth analog rotation.
  • Dynamic Limits Binding: Bind maximums dynamically via dynamicMaxValueMember or DynamicMinValueMember.
  • Integrated Precision Input Field: Includes a companion numeric text field (DrawInputField = true) for direct manual entry.
  • Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute for synthesizer arrays and mixer channels.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class SynthesizerChannel : MonoBehaviour
{
    [RotaryKnob(min: 0f, max: 100f, size: 48f)]
    public float lowPassFilterCutoff = 65f;
}

Animated Attributes/Spatial Controls/[SwipeActionRow]

[SwipeActionRow]

Renders a mobile-style swipeable list item where dragging horizontally reveals contextual quick-action buttons such as delete, reset, or inspect.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [SwipeActionRow] brings ubiquitous mobile list ergonomics into the Unity Inspector. By dragging an inspector row horizontally, the foreground property field slides to reveal an underlying contextual quick-action button (ActionButtonText, defaulting to "RESET" at 65px width) styled in a designated action color (ActionColor, defaulting to #EF4444 red). Clicking the revealed button executes an instant reset to default values with full Undo tracking. It also implements ICollectionElementAttribute for multi-item list cascading.

The Problem: Managing expansive serialized lists or arrays—such as inventory slots, dialogue trees, audio clips, or waypoint nodes—often clutters each row with redundant action buttons. Having permanent "Reset" or "Remove" buttons visible on every single line makes lists visually overwhelming.

The Solution: Decorating list elements or fields with [SwipeActionRow]:

  • Hidden Contextual Actions: Hides destructive or utility buttons beneath the row, keeping inspector lists clean until swiped.
  • Natural Touch/Drag Physics: Supports horizontal mouse dragging with momentum to expose or conceal action buttons.
  • Customizable Action Surface: Configure button label (ActionButtonText) and background color (ActionColor), such as red for delete, blue for duplicate, or amber for reset.
  • Universal Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute to effortlessly turn standard Unity lists and arrays into swipeable item tables.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class ProfileSettings : MonoBehaviour
{
    [SwipeActionRow(actionText: "DEFAULT", actionColor: "#EF4444")]
    public string usernameNickname = "PlayerOne";
}

Animated Attributes/Spatial Controls/[XYPadController]

[XYPadController]

Renders an interactive 2D touchpad joystick for Vector2 properties with crosshair grids, movable pucks, and optional spring recentering.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [XYPadController] replaces dual numeric float fields with a tactile 2D touchpad controller. It renders a square touch canvas (PadSize, defaulting to 80px) equipped with crosshairs and a draggable cursor puck (PuckColor, defaulting to hot pink #EC4899). Dragging the puck interactively maps normalized coordinates across MinValue and MaxValue (or dynamic bounds via DynamicMaxValueMember). It supports optional spring-return to origin upon release (SpringReturn = true), coordinate readouts (ShowValue), and collection cascading.

The Problem: Tuning two-dimensional vectors—such as virtual thumbstick deadzones, wind directions, texture UV offsets, aim offsets, or gravity direction—requires guessing raw X and Y numbers in separate float fields. Visualizing how X and Y relate spatially is difficult without a visual 2D coordinate plane.

The Solution: Decorating Vector2 properties with [XYPadController]:

  • Intuitive 2D Cartesian Pad: Drag the puck freely across 2D planar space to set X and Y coordinates simultaneously.
  • Elastic Spring Return: Optional snap-back to (0, 0) upon releasing the mouse (SpringReturn = true) for joystick simulation.
  • Dynamic Limits Binding: Bind maximum limits dynamically via dynamicMaxValueMember or DynamicMinValueMember.
  • Integrated Precision Input Field: Includes a companion Vector2 input field (DrawInputField = true) for direct manual entry.
  • Collection Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute strictly constrained to Vector2 elements.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class VirtualJoystick : MonoBehaviour
{
    [XYPadController(padSize: 90f, min: -1f, max: 1f)]
    public Vector2 analogStickInput = Vector2.zero;
}

        padSize: 100f,

    public Vector2 rightStickAimDirection = Vector2.zero;

    public List<Vector2> materialScrollTiling = new List<Vector2> { Vector2.one, new Vector2(2f, 2f) };
}

Animated Attributes/Transitions/[AccordionUnfold]

[AccordionUnfold]

Animates foldout groups and fields with smooth accordion height expansion and cross-fade opacity transitions.

Package: MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes Targets: Field | Property AllowMultiple: False Inherited: True Performance: High (Hardware-Accelerated UI Toolkit Transitions)

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [AccordionUnfold] replaces abrupt, jarring inspector visibility pop-ins with a fluid, natural accordion unfolding animation. When the decorated group, property, or collection element row attaches to the visual tree, the drawer measures the natural resolved height of the element, initializes maxHeight = 0 and opacity = 0, and smoothly expands downward over a configurable duration (UnfoldDuration, defaulting to 300ms) using a graceful Cubic Ease-Out curve (1 - (1 - t)³). It also supports optional cross-fade opacity transitions (FadeOpacity = true).

The Problem: Standard foldouts, conditional toggles ([ShowIf]), and collection item expansions in Unity snap open and shut instantaneously. This abrupt layout shifting jolts developer focus and feels archaic compared to modern fluid GUI frameworks.

The Solution: Decorating collapsible groups, conditional fields, or collection elements with [AccordionUnfold]:

  • Cubic Ease-Out Expansion: Interpolates height smoothly from zero to full natural resolved height with a deceleration curve.
  • Collection Element Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute so expanding collapsible collection element rows or arrays triggers smooth accordion unfolding.
  • Synchronized Opacity Fade: Cross-fades element opacity simultaneously (FadeOpacity = true) to prevent content clipping visually.
  • Automatic Layout Settlement: Upon reaching 100% progress, automatically resets maxHeight, opacity, and overflow to StyleKeyword.Null so dynamic child resizes remain unrestricted.
  • Zero Overhead When Resting: The schedule item terminates automatically upon completion, leaving 0% CPU consumption during steady-state inspection.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class CharacterCustomization : MonoBehaviour
{
    [AccordionUnfold(unfoldDuration: 350f)]
    public string advancedFacialMorphData = "Default_Preset_A";
}

3. Constructors & Parameters

unfoldDurationfloat300fExpansion and collapse transition duration in milliseconds.

Animated Attributes/Transitions/[CardFlip]

[CardFlip]

Animates a 3D card flip rotation around the Y-axis to alternate views or reveal advanced settings panels via button click.

Package: MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes Targets: Field | Property AllowMultiple: False Inherited: True Performance: High (Hardware-Accelerated UI Toolkit 3D Transforms)

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [CardFlip] introduces interactive 3D card rotation into inspector panels. It renders the property field alongside a stylized flip trigger button (flipBtn, styled with "🔄 " + ButtonLabel). When the developer clicks the button, the drawer triggers a 3D horizontal card flip rotation around the vertical Y-axis over a configurable duration (FlipDuration, defaulting to 350ms), compressing scaleX via a harmonic cosine curve (Mathf.Abs(Mathf.Cos(progress * Mathf.PI))) to simulate a double-sided card flip.

The Problem: Complex inspector components often have two distinct viewing needs: a simple high-level summary for level designers, and deep granular settings for technical leads. Having both simultaneously visible clutters the UI, while tab bars consume precious vertical screen space.

The Solution: Decorating dual-faceted configuration fields or list items with [CardFlip]:

  • Tactile 3D Flip Rotation: Simulates a card turning over around its vertical spine using hardware-accelerated UI Toolkit scale transforms.
  • Collection Element Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute so array and collection item rows gain dedicated flip action buttons to inspect individual card details.
  • Dedicated Flip Action Button: Renders a clean action button (ButtonLabel) right-aligned beside the property for instant flipping.
  • Configurable Flip Duration: Tune rotation tempo (FlipDuration) for snappy 200ms flips or deliberate 500ms showcase turns.
  • Zero Layout Breakage: Scale transforms modify visual rendering without altering layout flow or displacing adjacent controls.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class WeaponCard : MonoBehaviour
{
    [CardFlip(buttonLabel: "Flip for Calibration")]
    public float weaponOverheatThreshold = 85f;
}

Animated Attributes/Transitions/[ElasticPopIn]

[ElasticPopIn]

Pops inspector fields into view with an elastic overshoot spring scale animation upon initial mount or conditional reveal.

Package: MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes Targets: Field | Property AllowMultiple: False Inherited: True Performance: High (Hardware-Accelerated UI Toolkit Scale Transforms)

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [ElasticPopIn] introduces dynamic spring physics to inspector elements as they appear on screen. When an element or collection element row attaches to the panel, the drawer starts its scale at zero (Vector3.zero). After an optional stagger delay (Delay), the element scales up with an elastic overshoot trajectory (s = sin(p · (π) / (2)) + sin(p · π) · (Overshoot - 1)), springing outward past 1.0 up to a customizable peak multiplier (Overshoot, defaulting to 1.2x) before settling elastically at 1.0 over a designated timeframe (Duration, defaulting to 400ms).

The Problem: When toggling conditional properties, expanding new inventory slots, or switching tabs, newly revealed properties appear abruptly with static immediacy. Without dynamic motion, UI state changes feel stiff and lack the polished tactile feedback found in AAA games.

The Solution: Decorating conditionally revealed fields, new components, or collection elements with [ElasticPopIn]:

  • Physical Spring Overshoot: Scales outward past 100% up to Overshoot (e.g. 1.2x) before rebounding smoothly to rest.
  • Collection Element Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute so adding new slots or viewing collection elements pops each item in with spring physics.
  • Configurable Stagger Delays: Chain multiple fields together with progressive Delay offsets (e.g. 0ms, 50ms, 100ms) for choreographed domino pop-in effects.
  • Hardware-Accelerated UI Scale: Modifies StyleScale directly, keeping inspector performance fluid and smooth.
  • Graceful Lifecycle Cleanup: Terminates scheduling automatically once progress >= 1.0f is reached, ensuring zero ongoing CPU usage.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class SkillUnlockReward : MonoBehaviour
{
    [ElasticPopIn(duration: 400f, overshoot: 1.25f, delay: 0f)]
    public string newlyUnlockedSkill = "Meteor Swarm";
}

overshootfloat1.2fPeak scale multiplier reached during the outward spring rebound.
delayfloat0fStagger delay in milliseconds before the pop animation begins.

4. Properties & Customization Table

5. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.Runtime;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

namespace MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes.Sample
{
    public class ElasticPopInExample : ModularMonoBehaviour
    {
        [Title("ElasticPopIn Attribute Showcase", "Joyful spring scale pop-in transition")]
        [InfoBox("ElasticPopIn applies a playful, tactile scale pop-in animation with an elastic overshoot.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]

        [BoxGroup("1. Standalone Elastic Pop-In Fields")]
        [ElasticPopIn(duration: 400f, overshoot: 1.3f, delay: 0f)]
        public string victoryCrownBadge = "GRAND MASTER CHAMPION TROPHY";

        [ElasticPopIn(duration: 350f, overshoot: 1.25f, delay: 80f)]
        public float rewardGoldMultiplier = 3.5f;

        [BoxGroup("2. Inside Class Demonstration")]
        public AchievementCelebrationSlot primaryCelebration = new AchievementCelebrationSlot();

        [BoxGroup("3. Quest Rewards Manifest")]
        [InfoBox("ElasticPopIn cascades down to collections, applying spring scale pop-in animations with overshoot physics across each slot.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]
        [Title("Milestone Badges (Elastic Pop-In Cascading)")]
        [ElasticPopIn(duration: 380f, overshoot: 1.25f, delay: 50f)]
        public int[] milestoneLevelUnlocks = new int[] { 10, 25, 50, 75, 100 };

        public List<AchievementCelebrationSlot> questRewards = new List<AchievementCelebrationSlot>
        {
            new AchievementCelebrationSlot { achievementTitle = "Dungeon Delver Master", gemsAwarded = 100 },
            new AchievementCelebrationSlot { achievementTitle = "Flawless Boss Conqueror", gemsAwarded = 500 }
        };

        [Serializable]
        public class AchievementCelebrationSlot
        {
            public string achievementTitle = "Dragon Slayer Supreme";

            [ElasticPopIn(duration: 450f, overshoot: 1.35f, delay: 240f)]
            public int gemsAwarded = 250;
        }

        [BoxGroup("4. Pop-In Animation Controls")]
        [Button("Award Massive Celebration Bonus", BackgroundColor = "#F59E0B", TextColor = "#1C1102")]
        public void AwardBonus()
        {
            rewardGoldMultiplier += 0.5f;
            primaryCelebration.gemsAwarded += 50;
            Debug.Log($"<color=#FBBF24>[ElasticPopIn]</color> Celebration triggered! GoldMult: {rewardGoldMultiplier}x, Gems: {primaryCelebration.gemsAwarded}.");
        }
    }
}

Animated Attributes/Transitions/[StaggerCascade]

[StaggerCascade]

Animates fields or list items cascading into view sequentially with an automatic staggered delay and cubic ease-out slide-in motion.

Package: MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes Targets: Field | Property AllowMultiple: False Inherited: True Performance: High (Hardware-Accelerated UI Toolkit Transforms)

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [StaggerCascade] introduces choreographed arrival motion to inspector fields, tabs, or expanded list items. When a decorated element mounts to the panel (via initial display or parent foldout expansion), its initial opacity is set to zero (opacity = 0) and offset downward by a configurable pixel delta (SlideOffset, defaulting to 20px). Using an incremental element counter, each successive field or collection element row is assigned a progressive delay (index * CascadeDelay), then transitions smoothly into place using a hardware-accelerated cubic ease-out curve (t = 1 - (1 - progress)³) over ItemDuration (defaulting to 300ms).

The Problem: When opening foldouts, switching tab groups, or expanding collection lists, dense inspectors instantly flood the screen with dozens of controls simultaneously. This visual pop lacks organic pacing, feels rigid, and makes it harder for designers to parse complex configuration screens.

The Solution: Decorating sequential fields or collections with [StaggerCascade]:

  • Choreographed Domino Wave: Progressively reveals fields or collection items one after another using automated sequential index calculations.
  • Collection Element Cascading: Implements ICollectionElementAttribute so array and list items cascade gracefully into view in a staggered waterfall sequence.
  • Natural Cubic Ease-Out: Employs a physics-like decelerating trajectory (t = 1 - (1 - p)³) that slides upward smoothly to rest.
  • Adjustable Spatial Offset: Configurable slide distance (SlideOffset) allowing subtle 10px slips or dramatic 40px stage entrances.
  • Zero Ongoing Overhead: The internal UI Toolkit scheduler automatically shuts down as soon as each individual item reaches 100% progress (progress >= 1f).

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class SkillTreeConfig : MonoBehaviour
{
    [StaggerCascade(cascadeDelay: 50f, slideOffset: 25f)]
    public string primarySkillName = "Meteor Shower";

    [StaggerCascade(cascadeDelay: 50f, slideOffset: 25f)]
    public float baseDamage = 120f;

    [StaggerCascade(cascadeDelay: 50f, slideOffset: 25f)]
    public float manaCost = 45f;
}

3. Constructors & Parameters

csharp

ParameterTypeDefaultDescription

slideOffsetfloat20fVertical pixel offset from which the element slides up into place.

4. Properties & Customization Table

PropertyTypeDefaultDescription

SlideOffsetfloat20fInitial downward pixel offset prior to sliding in.

Orderint0Execution order relative to other modular inspector drawers.

5. Production-Grade Example

csharp
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.Runtime;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

namespace MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes.Sample
{
    public class StaggerCascadeExample : ModularMonoBehaviour
    {
        [Title("StaggerCascade Attribute Showcase", "Sequential staggered cascade animation")]
        [InfoBox("StaggerCascade calculates automatic incremental delays for consecutive fields or list entries, producing an elegant cascading waterfall entrance.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]

        [BoxGroup("1. Standalone Staggered Cascade Group")]
        [StaggerCascade(cascadeDelay: 40f, slideOffset: 20f, ItemDuration = 300f)]
        public int heroCombatPowerRating = 18450;

        [StaggerCascade(cascadeDelay: 80f, slideOffset: 20f, ItemDuration = 300f)]
        public string elementalAffinity = "Radiant Celestial Holy Fire";

        [StaggerCascade(cascadeDelay: 120f, slideOffset: 20f, ItemDuration = 300f)]
        public float maxManaCapacity = 2400f;

        [BoxGroup("2. Inside Class Demonstration")]
        public TalentTreeNode primaryTalent = new TalentTreeNode();

        [BoxGroup("3. Cascade Skill Bar Manifest")]
        [InfoBox("StaggerCascade cascades to collection elements, giving each entry an index-based staggered delay.", InspectorMessageType.Info)]
        [StaggerCascade(cascadeDelay: 40f, slideOffset: 15f)]
        public int[] hotbarAbilityIds = new int[] { 101, 102, 103, 104, 105 };

        public List<TalentTreeNode> activeTalentBuild = new List<TalentTreeNode>
        {
            new TalentTreeNode { branchName = "Pyromancy Mastery", pointsSpent = 25 },
            new TalentTreeNode { branchName = "Cryomancy Barrier", pointsSpent = 15 },
            new TalentTreeNode { branchName = "Chronomancy Haste", pointsSpent = 40 }
        };

        [Serializable]
        public class TalentTreeNode
        {
            public string branchName = "Ascendant Archmage";

            [StaggerCascade(cascadeDelay: 50f, slideOffset: 15f)]
            public int pointsSpent = 35;
        }

        [BoxGroup("4. Staggered Cascade Controls")]
        [Button("Allocate +5 Talent Points Across Build", BackgroundColor = "#38BDF8", TextColor = "#082F49")]
        public void AllocatePoints()
        {
            heroCombatPowerRating += 500;
            primaryTalent.pointsSpent += 5;
            for (int i = 0; i < activeTalentBuild.Count; i++)
            {
                activeTalentBuild[i].pointsSpent += 2;
            }
            Debug.Log($"<color=#38BDF8>[StaggerCascade]</color> Points allocated! Hero Power Rating: {heroCombatPowerRating}.");
        }
    }
}

Animated Attributes/Kinetic Typography/AcidCorrosionText

AcidCorrosionText

[AcidCorrosionText] · Target: Field | Property · Category: Typography · Assembly: MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes

1. Overview & Value Proposition

In toxic wastelands, alien bio-threats, chemical manufacturing, or weapon durability systems, standard inspector fields feel detached from the hazardous materials they represent. Static hazard text does not communicate volatile chemical corrosiveness.

[AcidCorrosionText] renders text in radioactive lime-green and sulfur-yellow hues, animating individual characters with an asymmetrical downward acid droplet droop and caustic horizontal micro-sizzles. Letters stretch viscously before snapping back, giving the visceral impression of boiling acid.

2. Quick-Start Snippet

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class ChemicalReservoir : MonoBehaviour
{
    [AcidCorrosionText(4.5f, 3.0f, "#84CC16")]
    public float FluoroantimonicMolarity = 18.5f;

Animated Attributes/Kinetic Typography/[AnimatedOdometer]

[AnimatedOdometer]

Displays numeric fields with an animated rolling odometer reel effect, live value scrubbing, and luminous value-change glow.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [AnimatedOdometer] upgrades standard numeric input fields with an animated mechanical odometer ticker. Whenever a serialized numeric value changes—whether through script changes, Undo/Redo operations, or inspector editing—the displayed digits roll fluidly to the new value using a cubic ease-out curve over RollDuration (defaulting to 400ms). While rolling, the container frame blooms with a customizable luminous highlight (GlowColor, defaulting to sapphire #3B82F6). It supports interactive vertical drag scrubbing (IsEditable), prefix/suffix formatting (e.g. "$" or " XP"), and flexible companion input field rendering (DrawInputField).

The Problem: Standard numeric float and int fields in Unity update instantly with zero visual transition. When inspecting fluctuating live values, balancing currency rates, or scrubbing health pools, static numbers make it difficult to perceive the direction and velocity of changes.

The Solution: Decorating numeric fields with [AnimatedOdometer]:

  • Rolling Reel Simulation: Numbers roll smoothly toward target values via a cubic ease-out interpolation function (v = v_0 + Δ v · (1 - (1 - p)³)).
  • Interactive Drag Scrubbing: Click and drag vertically over the odometer badge to rapidly increment or decrement values.
  • Dynamic Change Glow: Emits a bright luminous glow around the badge container while values are in motion.
  • Prefix & Suffix Support: Affixes currency symbols, units of measurement, or custom markers without altering the underlying raw numeric type.
  • Collection Cascading: Full collection element support via ICollectionElementAttribute for integer and floating-point collections.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;

{
    [AnimatedOdometer(rollDuration: 500f, Prefix = "$", Suffix = " USD", GlowColor = "#10B981")]
    public float balance = 1250.75f;
}

Animated Attributes/Kinetic Typography/BinaryStreamText

BinaryStreamText

[BinaryStreamText] · Target: Field | Property · Category: Typography · Assembly: MSUltimate-AnimatedAttributes

1. Overview & Value Proposition

In sci-fi, cyberpunk, or modern terminal interfaces, static field labels feel rigid and unresponsive. Standard Unity inspector labels cannot communicate real-time digital computation, network handshakes, or cryptographic ciphers.

[BinaryStreamText] transforms any field label into a live digital stream. Individual characters intermittently flicker into raw bits ('0', '1') or hexadecimal bytes ('0'–'F') before resolving back into readable text. Hovering or clicking the field triggers an interactive sequential sweep wave across all characters, delivering immediate visual tactility to technical gameplay parameters.

2. Quick-Start Snippet

csharp

    public string UplinkCipher = "SEC_ALPHA_NODE_99";
}

Animated Attributes/Kinetic Typography/[BouncingText]

[BouncingText]

Applies a springy letter-by-letter staggered bounce animation to field labels on hover or click.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [BouncingText] brings tactile feedback to inspector typography by segmenting field labels into individual character glyphs. When the user hovers over the field (MouseEnterEvent) or clicks to focus (PointerDownEvent), it triggers a choreographed wave bounce across the letters. Each consecutive character begins its bounce after a brief offset (StaggerDelay, defaulting to 35ms), ascending upward by BounceHeight (defaulting to 4px) along a sinusoidal trajectory (y = -sin(progress · π) · BounceHeight) while temporarily shifting to a warm accent color (BounceColor, defaulting to amber #F59E0B).

The Problem: Inspectors packed with dozens of monotonous text labels feel static and uninviting. Important fields—such as easter eggs, critical alerts, or promotional rewards—blend seamlessly into generic inspector panels without any subtle visual personality.

The Solution: Decorating prominent properties with [BouncingText]:

  • Interactive Micro-Animation: Rewards user interaction with immediate, springy kinetic feedback on mouse hover or click.
  • Sinusoidal Wave Trajectory: Produces a fluid Mexican-wave hop across the text without jagged frame steps.
  • Dynamic Color Accents: Characters temporarily adopt a vibrant highlight hue (BounceColor) at peak bounce height.
  • Graceful Lifecycle Management: Executes only during active bounces; automatically shuts down animation scheduling when all letters return to rest.

2. Quick Start

csharp
using UnityEngine;
using MaharajaStudio.MSUltimate.AnimatedAttributes;

public class SpecialRewardChest : MonoBehaviour
{
    [BouncingText(bounceHeight: 5f, staggerDelay: 30f, BounceColor = "#FBBF24")]
    public string legendaryItemDrop = "Excalibur";
}

SpecificationValue

Supported TypesAny serializable field or property
Allow MultipleNo (AllowMultiple = false)
InheritedYes
Collection SupportSupported on fields in wrapper classes (List<MyClass>); Direct collection cascading is intentionally not supported.
UI Toolkit CompatibleYes

Animated Attributes/Kinetic Typography/[ChronoStopwatch]

[ChronoStopwatch]

Displays an interactive digital chronograph stopwatch widget with real-time play/pause/reset controls directly within the inspector.

1. Overview & Value Proposition

What it does: [ChronoStopwatch] turns raw numerical timing fields (representing seconds or milliseconds) into a fully functional digital chronograph. It renders a modern HUD stopwatch panel (#10131A) featuring digital readouts in cyber cyan (DigitColor, defaulting to #38BDF8) formatted with minute, second, and optional millisecond precision (00:00.00). Equipped with interactive transport buttons—Start/Pause (▶ / ⏸) and Stop/Reset (⏹)—developers and level designers can benchmark physics durations, measure cooldown intervals, or record gameplay sequences directly inside the inspector.

The Problem: Tuning duration values—such as stun durations, cutscene timers, cooldown delays, or speedrun splits—traditionally involves guessing raw numbers, entering Play Mode, and checking external stopwatches or console logs to verify real-world feel.

The Solution: Decorating numeric time fields with [ChronoStopwatch]:

  • Integrated Transport Controls: Start, pause, resume, and zero-out timers with single-click UI buttons directly beside serialized fields.
  • Bi-Directional Property Binding: Automatically pushes stopwatch accumulated time into the underlying serialized property, recording full Undo/Redo transactions.
  • Millisecond Precision: Toggles between standard minute/second (00:00) and high-resolution millisecond (00:00.00) displays via ShowMilliseconds.
  • Standalone or Companion Mode: Keep the standard numeric text field (DrawInputField = true) or replace it entirely with the chronograph widget (DrawInputField = false).
  • Collection Support: Full support for numeric lists and arrays via ICollectionElementAttribute.

2. Quick Start
